  Limited by Shares (Ltd)", "Limited Liability Company (LLC)", a currency
  code). Stored values never change for a wording; documents keep full
  names.
- **Exception: official and legal names stay as written**, brackets and
  all: legal forms ("Toiminimi (sole proprietorship)", "Corporation
  (federal)", "Empresario individual (autónomo)", "Stichting
  (foundation)", "Unternehmergesellschaft (UG, haftungsbeschränkt)"), a
  tax's official name ("VAT (USt)") and a platform's name ("X
  (Twitter)").

### Tracking follows weight

Text that goes bold under the pointer (`--hover-bold`) gets 0.05em of tracking ON TOP of what the element already has (`--bold-tracking` added to `--rest-tracking`: a button's 0.1em, none elsewhere), as the Capability and System list always did; text that is bold by default has the same. Spacing comes with bold only: the lit rail item is wider than the others, never tighter. A bullet under the pointer is only underlined. Every CSS rule that sets a bold weight (600 and up) or `--hover-bold` sets `letter-spacing: calc(var(--rest-tracking, 0em) + var(--bold-tracking))` with it, unless it states its own tracking (the uppercase labels keep theirs); `strong`, `b`, `th` and `h1`-`h6` carry it by default. A glyph (a chip's ×) is not text and takes none. A row that holds bold text on hover must be sized for the wider text (one line, no re-wrap).

### The label arrow

One fold arrow (`FoldArrow.tsx`, `label-arrow.css`) for an arrow that stands beside a label or a text and shows or hides what belongs to it.
Today: the chapter titles of 3. Infrastructure & Capability (right of the title and its "i"), and, left of the label and inside it, a founder's Responsibilities and Completed, a card's Links and the Initiatives of an Objective.
- No frame and no ground: only the triangle. At rest an outline in the card's tint (the "+" box's colour); under the pointer and the keyboard focus the same triangle filled.
- The shape is the Completed arrow's (11px wide and 18px high pointing right, 18 x 11px pointing down, at an 18px label); the size scales with the label's letters (1em high). The line is 2px, inside the shape, like the "+" box's. The colour is the card's tint (the "+" box's).
- Inside a label its glyph starts at the content edge, like a tick box, and the words start where a tick box's words start (26px). The button is never under 24px; it overhangs the triangle and takes no more room.
- Only these use it: the chapter titles of 3. Infrastructure & Capability, the Initiatives, a founder's Responsibilities and Completed, and a card's Links. Every other fold arrow keeps the standard 36px arrow (`FoldArrow`).

---

## 7. Lists, grids and card layouts

### Grids and lists

- **Page grids** (`.answer-columns`: Identity's, Strategy's; `.brand-style` /
  `.brand-inventory`: Brand's; `.legal-entity-body` and `.legal-entity-row`:
  Legal Entity): columns `--space-column`, rows `--space-field`, stacked
  section boxes `--space-card`: 12px every way. A Brand Inventory image and
  its preview are `--space-cell` apart.
- A control starts at its column's left edge; controls of one row share
  their top edge; side-by-side boxes share their bottom edge.
- A list with a x keeps the x column on every row (`32px` track), so the
  columns before it stay put.
- An add button at a list's end spans the list ("+ ADD ...", labelled).
- **List-end add button**: "+ ADD ..." under a list, the list's whole width,
  its label centred (`.legal-entity-list-add`): registrations, tax
  registrations, founders.
- **Free-text numbers wrap**: a registration or tax number, a VAT number, a
  requirement's reference are free text, not figures: they keep their
  standard width and wrap onto more lines, growing down; the x and the
  fields beside them stay on the first line.
- **Tick rows**: a tick that stands as a field ("Add to Legal Entity
  Expenses", "Relevant Information") is one line, a row gap from what is
  above and below it, and a label gap above the box it opens. The options
  of one tick list (several ticks under one label: a Legal Entity expense's
  Links) are rows of that field, each one line and a row gap (12px,
  `--space-field`) from the next: the tick-to-tick pitch is the same in a
  list as between two tick fields. **Every tick-to-tick gap in the app is that
  12px** (`--space-field`): a stack of tick options (the Links, a field's
  Functional Needs), a row of ticks (Goals, Demand), the Brand Style
  columns' slots and rows, and a tick field above or below another; a tick
  row has no min-height of its own beside the 45px of a row that holds a
  field (a tick row in a Brand Style column keeps 45px so the columns share
  a line; the gap between row boxes is still 12px). The probe checks
  `.legal-entity-links` and a field's stacked tick options.
- **Activity cards: Completed and the Links** (the owner's rule): a **divider line** stands above the Completed tick, 12px (`--space-field`) of space above and below it, and the **Links** ("Linked to", the Offerings the activity is linked to) come last, **after Completed**. Wherever an Activity card is opened (Infrastructure, Offering's Capability, the Completed tile) the order is: the fields, Description, the divider, Completed, the Links. (The library entries: docs/design/library/02-assets-library.md, DIV.001, CRD.003, CRD.007.)
- **Navigation rails** (`bullet-rail.css`, wide windows): the two rails are
  mirrors of each other and share every rule: the name 18px over its list, rows
  18px apart, the dot 20px from the card, the text 12px
  (`gap`) from the dot, both rails (the dot is 12px wide: the name 24px past
  the dot's edge). The name's text edge is its list's INK edge: the right edge of the
  Pages labels (right-aligned beside their dots), the left edge of the
  Sections numbers' ink: the right rail's label is pulled left by
  `--rail-number-lead` (the blank the right-aligned number column leaves
  before a one-digit "N."), so the number's INK is 12px from the dot and the
  name's padding is plain 24px.
- **Founder card** (Founders Profile): on the card's two equal columns,
  the Roles in the first column of the first row (the second empty), then
  Industry | Management, then Achievements | Skills; one column under a
  600px card.
- **Objective card** (Strategy): on the columns of its Initiatives | Metrics
  row; the Description in the first (as wide as the Initiatives box); in
  the second, from the Metric column's edge, "Start [month]" over "End
  [month]" (the Strategic Period's pair: the same month picker, its
  label in a 48px column beside it, the Date step) and, as a third row
  under the End box, "Responsible": the Capability cards' own side-label
  row (`BusinessField side`, see "Capability cards: short side labels": the
  label in `--side-label-short`, `--side-label-gap`, a hugging dropdown, one
  centre line), a field gap (12px) under End. "Start" stands on the
  Description label's line. With exactly one founder (or none) there is no
  Responsible row at all (no label, no text, no empty row; the rows reflow
  at the 12px rhythm) and the objective is attributed to the sole founder
  wherever it is read (`strategyObjectiveOwnerId`). One column under 900px. Its columns, and every box inside the card, stand
  `--space-card` (12px) apart.
- **A Metric box's x under its Objective's filter (funnel) button.** The
  Metric box ends where that column needs it, and its name and
  description boxes end with it. (Every card's own x stays at its own
  12px side margin: a Goal's x is not moved in to its Objectives'.)
- **A Metric's two rows of figures** (Frequency | Unit, Baseline | Target,
  `.strategy-metric-fields`: every Metric, in a Theme's dialog and in a
  Goal's floating card): two columns sized by their content (owner,
  2026-11-09). Each column is at least as wide as its widest picker needs
  for its longest option (the Frequency's "Semiannually"; the Unit's
  "Percentage", with the currency picker beside a Currency Unit), and what
  the row has over is shared between the two (`repeat(2, auto)`). Every
  control fills its column (the closed set's "its column"), so the
  Baseline stands under the Frequency at its width and the Target under
  the Unit; the number cells ask nothing of the columns (`width: 0;
  min-width: 100%`). Where the row is narrower than its pickers need, the
  columns give way from equal shares.
- **Segment card** (Business Foundation, the page as tiles): a Segment's Business Foundation subchapter is four tiles, two by two (Segment Description, Value Proposition, Competitors, Offerings), beside its Engagement subchapter (section 13, "Foundation & Engagement, one Segment at a time"); its Description opens in a dialog and a Profile, a Competitor or an Offering floats over it. The Description is laid out as the Positioning card is: two equal columns with a 1px divider between, each box a title band on the primary fill, each question a band under it (every other one tinted), the shared double rule between one box and the next, one column under 900px; the boxes are shared between the columns by their height. A Segment of businesses has Business Data (before Geographics) and no Demographics; where Positioning chose both B2C and B2B the first two Segments are made automatically (people, then businesses). Profiles are a section under the boxes: the shared double rule, a title band in the boxes' style, the Profile tiles in a band under it. The questions and their layout are in Questions 12; the Profile card, a floating card of questions, is in section 8, "A floating card of questions". A Segment is added with "+ Add Segment". **The frame's title** (the Segment's name, typed in place: `SegmentName`) is a title as every tile title is (section 9, "Tile title" and the slide under it): one line cut by an ellipsis, the full name its tooltip, sliding to its end under the pointer and back on leave (`useTitleSlide`; still while it is typed in, home again when left); it ends 12px before the x and never under it (the head's padding on both sides: the x's 4px, its 32px and the 12px gap, so the title stays centred).
- **Competitor and Offering cards**: the Type field is a row of the head
  under the name, set against the name box's RIGHT edge: the picker's
  right edge is the name box's right edge. "Type" is a compact side label
  beside its picker on the picker's mid line, 12px under the name and
  12px above the body. The label and the picker keep their sizes (the
  picker hugs its longest option, see the hug exception); the pair is
  right-aligned.
- **Customer Engagement** (the Channel table, the Channel resources table, the action markers, Sales Forecast): see section 11, from "The Customer Engagement page" on.
- **Additional Legal Requirement card**: Reference or Number | Type on one
  row of the card's two columns, the Type at the width its longest option
  needs.
- **Name / Number list** (`--name-number-columns`): the name the rest, the
  number S (two fifths of a narrow list), the x column. Names wrap and grow;
  the number and x stay on the name's first line. Used by the Legal
  Entity's Formation & Registration and Tax & Business Registration, so
  their Number columns share one x. Column heads head only the rows the user
  adds; rows that name themselves (the tax number, VAT) have none.
- **Founders table**: Name the rest (wraps and grows), Ownership % the XS
  step, Capital the Code step, the x column on every row, `--space-cell`
  apart. Heads are field labels, on one line, centred over their figures
  ("Ownership %" is wider than XS and reaches into the gaps beside it).
  Total Capital, then the Total Ownership badge, under the table from its
  left edge. A block per founder under 700px.
- **Side-label row snapped to the grid** (`.legal-entity-side-row`): a row
  of fields whose labels sit beside their controls on the columns of the
  list below (a subgrid): each label at its column's left edge, its control
  filling the rest of the column (so it ends where the column ends), labels
  on the controls' centre line. Where a column cannot hold its label and
  its control's longest value, the labels go above. Status | Date on
  Formation & Registration. The Status field's left edge (its label, then
  its picker) is the card body's left edge, so the "Status" label aligns
  with the "Authority / Name" label below it; the label stands
  `--side-label-gap` (12px) before its picker, which hugs its longest
  option (never truncated).
- **Capability cards: short side labels** (Business System, 3. Infrastructure &
  Capability). Frequency on a Resource card (at Costs) and Responsible on a Partner /
  Provider card are `BusinessField side` rows (an Activity's Responsible keeps its own
  side label, and a Partner / Provider's own Frequency is unchanged: its label above its
  dropdown, beside the Price): the label in the one short column (`--side-label-short`,
  177px, the width of the tick, "Responsible" and its "i"), `--side-label-gap` (12px), then the
  dropdown, which HUGS its longest option (the hug exception), the label centred on the
  dropdown's centre line (`align-items:center`). The two dropdowns therefore start at the
  same x (the card body's left + 164 + 12). A Resource's Frequency row spans the fields
  under Qty | Price | currency; a Partner / Provider's Responsible row spans the card's
  fields under its Price | Frequency pair; under 340px (the fields' own box) the label goes
  above its dropdown. A Partner / Provider's field is labelled "Responsible" (its field key
  is still "responsibility"); a Strategy Objective's Responsible is this same row. In an
  organisation with exactly one founder the Responsible / Responsibility field is NOT shown
  at all -- on an Activity, a Partner / Provider and a Strategy Objective: no label, no
  text, no empty row (a folded Activity does not name him either); he is still responsible
  where it is read (`businessAutoResponsibleId`, `businessPartnerResponsibleId`,
  `strategyObjectiveOwnerId`: documents, exports, Review). With several founders or none it
  is unchanged.
  **The Responsible tick**: with two or more founders the Responsible / Responsibility label
  carries a tick box NEXT TO it (`BusinessField tick`: `label.business-foundation-tick` =
  the `brand-tick-box` then the label's words, centred on the label's first line; the "i"
  after the label, outside it), ticked by default. The tick, the words and the "i" are ONE
  line (`white-space:nowrap`; the "i" 6px (`--info-gap`) after the last word, all on one centre line), in the
  short side-label column beside the dropdown and in a stacked narrow card alike, never split
  across lines. Unticking removes the field for that card
  (an Activity, a Partner / Provider, a Strategy Objective): the dropdown goes, the tick and
  the label stay so it can be ticked again, the stored choice is cleared and
  `responsibleOff: true` is stored on the card (the key absent = ticked, so no stored answer
  changes). An unticked card means "no responsible by choice": it is not flagged in Review /
  Open Items, the sole-founder / single-Team rules do not attribute it (`responsibleIsOff`,
  read through `businessResponsibleId`, `businessPartnerResponsibleId`,
  `strategyObjectiveOwnerId`), and exports / documents leave it empty. With one founder there
  is no field and so no tick.
  **Folded Capability cards are one fixed size** (Resource, Activity, Partner / Provider:
  `fixedFolded`, `.fold-card-head-fixed`): the name on ONE line cut by an ellipsis (the full
  name is its tooltip) and the summary on one line under it, cut the same way and kept when
  empty, so every folded card in a list has the same height. Open, the name box wraps and
  grows as on every card.
  **4. Delivery cards** carry no static paragraph: the explanations sit in "i" notes (on
  "Working Hours per Unit, by person" and on the capacity line). Delivered, Hours Given and
  Calendar Time are side-label rows (`BusinessField side`, one label column wide enough for
  "Calendar Time (weeks)", the control hugging, one centre line; the label above in a card
  narrower than that), a person's row is [name ... hours box] with the XS boxes on one right
  edge and the box on the first line's centre of a wrapped name, the Total under the rows
  and the computed panel (compact labelled lines, 12px padding) 12px apart (the effort stack).
  A person's hours box has no unit after it (its name says hours).

### Body order

Fields follow the order a founder answers them: what it is, then where,
then the details. A label and its cell standing side by side (the Profile's age) have no divider line under the label: the line is for a label over its options. The Organisation dialog of the Identity System (the Definition chapter) reads in two columns, where it is (Name, Country, Address) on the left and what it is (Legal Form, the dates, the activities) on the right; the fields and their order are Questions 7, and the dialog is `specifications/01-identity-system/01-definition.md`, section 4. Its measures, kept here: the Main Activity's code is always a tick row exactly 12px under the Main Activity box (unticked, just the tick; ticked, its box, 45px high, beside the words on their centre line, the Code step wide, six digits fit, at the column's right edge; a saved code loads ticked); the Legal Form takes its column's width, every UK and US form read in full (the longest, 57 characters, fits from a 1366px window; a closed picker too narrow ends in "..."); the Address is one box of lines, its placeholder the lines the country expects in its own words ("Street", "City", "Postcode" / "ZIP Code" / "PLZ"; no postal line where addresses carry none).

### The Review page

The Review (`OrganizationReview.tsx`, `review.css`; the Workbook page's
Review tab since 2026-11-09, "The Workbook" below) stands in the Workbook's
one column (`.glossary-page`),
every gap and corner a token (`--gap-24` between the Organisations and
between the groups, `--space-field` under a head, `--gap-8` in a group,
`--card-padding` in a row, `--radius-md` on a list). An Organisation's head
and a group's head are both the shared `FoldHead` with no arrow, so there
is ONE count style (`.fold-head-count`); a group's count that only repeats
the Organisation's (the one group counted; the key dates are never counted)
is left out. A Capability's sub-head stands only where a group spans more
than one Capability. The total review (the Workbook opened from the Landing
page) is the Landing page's: its header carries no Organisation's name or
tools, only the Workbook button, whatever Organisation was selected last. Where
there are two or more Organisations it has the Glossary's funnel
(`ResponsibilityFilter` in the `.glossary-bar`, at the end of the row
under the tabs; owner, 2026-11-09): one tick for each Organisation, all
ticked at first; one ticked off leaves its review out ("No Organisations
to show" when none is left). One Organisation's review has no funnel. The
items' wording and paths are as built (the owner's choice, still open).

### The Workbook

- **The Workbook is one page drawn as a dialog of the app** (no drop shadow on the panel or its rail's tab: a page of its own lifts off nothing; owner, 2026-11-10) (owner, 2026-11-09: "a whole page, not a side panel"; "Check an app's card (dialog) style: navigation arrows, side gaps, framing"): `/workbook` (`pages/Workbook.tsx`). The title "Workbook" at the top left over its rule, after its icon, the header button's own glyph at the System icons' size and weight (a closed notebook with a bookmark, no circle; `InterviewHeader` `icon`, `assets/workbook-icon.svg`), as wide as a System page's card (`--card-width`); **the panel's head stays while its body scrolls**: the section's heading and, at the right end of its row, the section's buttons (`WorkbookTools`): the Forecast's Period and Until (each label beside its picker, before the text size), the text size (the Inventory's three "Aa", the section drawn at 14, 18 or 24 to 18), the funnels, the funnels, and the Business Documents' one **Export** (`ExportMenu`: the download symbol and the word, a menu of the formats the document has, CSV for the Forecast, Markdown and PDF for all, in the sort menu's look, `MenuOption`); under it ONE panel drawn as a section dialog (`CardShell` with `section-tile-dialog`: its padding, ground and edge, in the Workbook's hue, `workHue("review")`), from the rule down to 12px over the footer, where the Back stands; the page itself never scrolls, so its title stays; the body scrolls, the Business Documents' tabs staying at its top. Its lists are bands (a card's note, a review item, a term, a reference: every other one on the 6% tint). In the panel: the section's name as its heading (level 2, `DialogHeading`, the "i" on Glossary, References and Business Documents), then the section. The sections go round with the carousel arrows at the panel's sides and the bullets of the tab attached to it (`CarouselArrows`, as on every dialog; Alt + the arrow keys), the tab always at the panel's left, as a System page's page tab (`side="left"`): the names beside the bullets where the window has room for them, else in their tooltips. **Sections:** Card Notes (`WorkbookCardNotes`: every card's Notes, its name and page on one line where there is room, a funnel of pages, a press opens the card), Review (`OrganizationReview`; inside an Organisation with no heading of its name, which the header says), Glossary (`GlossaryList`, ONE funnel: the kinds, then the letter ranges), References (the Harvard list, a funnel of letter ranges) and **Business Documents** (`BusinessDocumentsView`; its subsections are the tabs Business Plan, Business Model Canvas and Financial Forecast, `TabList`; the tabs and the chosen document are ONE page: the page (`business-documents-page`, the tab panel) hangs from the tabs' rule, framed on its other three sides in the card's edge, the chosen tab open into it, and holds the Plan, the Canvas or the Forecast; on screen a plan year is "Year 1", its months in the note and the tooltip, and the cover leaves out the "Organisation:" line its title already says; the exports print them in full). Opened by the header's `WorkbookButton` (no count; a closed notebook icon). On the Landing page the same button opens it with `total`: the Review of every Organisation (a funnel of the Organisations where there are two or more), the Glossary (every Organisation's terms, each named with its Organisation) and the References; the Card Notes and the Business Documents are one Organisation's and show inside it. `/glossary`, `/review` and `/documents` open it on their section (`WorkbookRedirect`), as do Search's terms and System Selection's Business Documents button; Back and Escape return to where it was opened from. **The Notes tool is as it was before the Workbook**: its own header tool after the Workbook (`NotepadButton`, the page icon, with the count of notes) opens and closes the side panel (`notepad-panel`, 360px: its head "Notes" with the closing arrow beside it and the dividing line under them, then "+ Add note" first under the line; the open Organisation's notes directly, with no heading (its name is the header's), another Organisation's and the General notes in their folding groups; "Link a field" picking over the page; its `NavArrow`, the Notes button and Escape close it; it stays open from page to page, the Workbook page too); the Calendar's notes open it on their note. Its closing arrow is drawn wherever it is open, a page of dialogs and the Workbook too. **Linking:** "Link a field" links a field, a card or a page's tile (a Master Tile, by its title: "# 2.1.1 Aesthetics"; a press anywhere on the tile, its list too, links it, and a tile does not open while picking); a page of tiles is never linked whole. A note's Date tick has its word at the body size, as every tick's.

---

## 8. Section tiles and dialog

3. Business System › Infrastructure & Capability draws its sections as
**tiles** (`SectionTiles.tsx`, `section-tiles.css`); the section itself is
drawn only in a dialog that its tile opens.

- **Tiles.** A true square, `--section-tile-size` wide and high
  (`aspect-ratio: 1 / 1`), one size in every group of a card. The size is
  **derived from the width the tiles have**, in CSS (no script): each group
  sits in a `.section-tiles-box` (`container-type: inline-size`), and
  `--section-tile-size` = (`100cqw` - gaps) / the number across, never under
  `--section-tile-min` (120px) and always counted as at least three across, so a group of up to three keeps the
  three-across size (about 215px at 1600px wide, 190px at 1366px) and one of four or more across shrinks to fit. The
  cards have no head and nothing in them folds, so there is no body indent: a 3 x 3 set exactly fills the card's content width.
  **Tile anatomy** (the tile is a two-row grid: the title's row and the rest): the title's **first line stands 24px from the tile's top**, 12px on a dialog's small tiles (`--tile-title-gap`; owner, 2026-11-09: so four lines of the list fit above the tile's foot), whatever the number of lines -- a long title grows downward, its rule and the list following its last line (the title's row, `--tile-title-top`, a quarter of the square) --
  **underlined** by a rule of its own (a 1px bottom border on the title element, in the title's colour at 40%, 4px under
  its last line), at most three lines (fewer where the room above the line holds fewer) then an ellipsis. A title too long for the room
  above its line (first line at least 12px from the square's top) drops to `--font-small` (14px), **that title only**
  (`TileTitle` measures it; the tile takes `data-title-small`). Under the rule, **12px** down (`--space-card`; the list moves up with the lifted title: `TileTitle` writes the tile's lift into `--tile-lift` and the list's top margin is `12px - --tile-lift`, so the gap from the rule to the list is 12px whatever the lift or the title's size),
  the **list of what the section includes** (`TileList`): small text (`--font-small`), `--text-muted` (5.56:1 at the
  least, sepia), the app's drawn bullet, one line per item cut by an ellipsis -- an item whose text carries a forced break (`\n`: "Cooling & Air" / "Conditioning", "Online Presence" / "& Communication", the only two) is TWO lines, each cut by its own ellipsis, the bullet on the first, and the text hangs under the text, not the bullet -- the list scrolls inside its bounds (there is no "+N more" line); a block as wide as its longest item (at most the tile's inner width, 12px in
  from each side), **centred under the title**, its lines left-aligned inside it; **a page's Master Tiles keep each list centred on the tile's centre line at its own width** (owner, 2026-11-20 21:28 and 21:32 UTC+2: "the master tiles listed items are centered aligned", his screenshot of Infrastructure & Capability with each tile's centre line); **in a dialog's grid the lists share one width**, the widest of them (its scrolled-away items too), still centred, so they start from one point in every tile of the grid (owner, 21:01: "the channels & Journey listed Items are not centered aligned, or at least they do not follow the same alignment"; 21:31: "create a point that depending on the length of the words, they would start from the same point"; `TileList`, `askToAlign`, `data-shared-width`; rule | was | now: one alignment for a dialog's tile lists | each list centred at its own width, so a row's lists began at different places (Journey Stages 13 to 53px in, Resources 13 to 27px) | one width a dialog grid, centred; the page's Master Tiles as before, 0px off their centre line); text (plain or centred lines, a column page's text) and two-column lists keep their own width; on a page tile every item is a real control (see
  "The page tile's slot"), and the tile's **accessible name** says it again: the title and the first six items joined by commas, cut at about
  140 characters ("Utilities, Electricity, Internet, Heating & Gas ..."). What each tile lists: the Founders tile each founder
  with their names alone, the Workspace tile its type or "Not set", Utilities / Workstation /
  Equipment / Digital Infrastructure their subcategory names, always (an added one too, and a subcategory that holds no card yet), an Activities tile its active
  activities, the partner tiles their partners and providers, Offering's Capability's tiles the names of their cards
  (Delivery: the Offerings), a subcategory box its cards' names. An EMPTY section (no cards yet) that has the "+" box shows no list and no glyph, only the box (the tile is still described as "No items yet"); an empty box WITHOUT a "+" box (a subcategory box in a dialog) keeps the small "+" (24px,
  `--font-h3`, the title's colour, decorative) where the list would be. Founders, the Workspace and Delivery are not lists of cards and never show either.
  The title is the body size (18px), weight 600 and the tracking 0.15em (the button's 0.1em + the bold 0.05em, as the app; 2026-11-19 reconciliation); the type scale is `--font-small` 14px,
  `--font-body` 18px, `--font-h3` 24px, `--font-h2` 28px, `--font-h1` 36px (see "Title sizes"). The longest word
  ("Infrastructure", 145px at 18px) fits a tile from about 171px wide; in a narrower tile a long word breaks inside the square
  (`overflow-wrap: anywhere`). **An "&" never ends a line**: in every tile, box and founder-box title it is joined to the word
  after it with a no-break space (`drawnTitle()`, drawn text only; the button's accessible name is the plain title and list).
  **No stroke and no border:** a box has no border at all (`border: 0`; the padding takes the pixel the border had, so every position inside it is where it was); its edge is an inset light from the top
  left and an inset shade to the bottom right (`--box-inset`: `inset 3px 3px 6px --box-light, inset -3px -3px 6px --box-shade`, per theme). The ground is the card's inner ground in the hue of
  the card the tile sits in; radius 8px (a card's). Hover keeps a 4px soft glow beside the inset; focus a 2px primary outline. The dashed add boxes keep their own edge. **Why no border:** a transparent 1px border left a hairline of the plain ground OUTSIDE the inset shadow (an inset shadow is drawn inside the border), light where the shade is and dark where the light is -- a thin line around the box in the opposite tones. Measured (luminance across the Utilities tile's four edges): outside 241 -> 240, 239, 236 ... 228 at the left and the top, 228 ... 202, 213, 241 at the right and the bottom -- one smooth step each, no line.
  Where a title still does not fit, it is cut after as many lines as stand above its line.
  Reactions: see "The page tile's slot" below and section 6.
- **The page tile's slot** (`TileSlot` in `SectionTiles.tsx`). A tile on the page is a SLOT -- the grid item, the tile's size -- that holds, all INSIDE the
  square: the tile's own button (the square: title, rule, the "+" of an empty section; its press opens the section's dialog), the **list of what it
  includes laid over the square** (`.section-tile-links-layer` on the tile's own rows, so the list stands exactly where it stood inside the
  button) and, on some tiles, the "+" box. A button cannot hold buttons, so the list is not inside the tile's button; it is a sibling over it, the
  layer taking no pointer events and its buttons taking theirs: a click on the tile outside an item is the tile's own. (The dialog's boxes --
  subcategory boxes, card boxes -- are plain buttons as before.)
  - **List items are controls.** Each item that leads somewhere is a `button.section-tile-link` (focusable, its own accessible name = its text),
    underlined under the pointer and the focus (`text-decoration-color` from transparent to the text colour, offset 3px; focus: a 2px primary outline
    inside the row). Pressing it opens THAT item ALONE as a **floating card** (see below): a founder's name that founder's card, an activity /
    resource / partner / provider the card's editor, A subcategory name (Utilities > Electricity ...) opens its ONE card alone, floating, when it holds exactly one; the section's dialog ON that subcategory when it holds several (a grid is not a card; Back returns to the boxes); and, empty, the dialog on it, where a blank card is made and waits (the first-open rule). Delivery's
    list stays plain text, hidden from assistive technology, as it was.
  - **The reaction (hover).** Under the pointer ONLY -- not with the focus: a tile that holds the focus has its ring -- **the tile stays in its cell at its own size** (owner, 2026-11-23 16:21, 17:52 and 18:32 UTC+2: the Brand tile, "Fix the direction the Tiles scale up", the Strategy page's SWOT tile, "There is a bug here"; rule | was | now: no tile covers its neighbours | the slot grew to 110% about its corner (`scaleOrigin()`) and overlapped the tiles beside it | no growth): its shadow lifts (`--shadow-tile-lift`, `transition: box-shadow .15s`), it stands above its neighbours (`z-index: 6`) and its pointer says it can be pressed (04-tile-slot-and-floating-card.css). **A page opened under a resting pointer** lifts nothing until the pointer moves (`PointerStill`, `data-pointer-still` on the root). This does NOT apply to the boxes in a dialog. Hovering the "+" box
    or a list item is hovering the tile. **Closing returns to the default page:** after a dialog or a floating card closes (Escape, Back, a click outside) no tile is grown even if the pointer rests on one (`data-tiles-settled` on the root, until the pointer moves 3px), and the focus comes back to the opener WITHOUT a ring after a mouse click (`data-focus-quiet`, until the keyboard is used); after the keyboard (Enter) it shows its ring, and still no tile grows. The real-mouse check (`checkRealMouse` in `run.mjs`) presses every list item of every first-row tile at the top of the page in a tall and a low window and tests all of this (the old code fails it with 38 failures at 1600 x 780).
  - **The "+" box** (`.plus-box`, on a page tile `.section-tile-plus`) is on the tiles that take new cards, and only on them: Infrastructure's Operational, Production, Marketing, Sales & Customer
    (and any category tile the founder added), Partners & Suppliers, **Utilities, Workstation, Equipment and Digital Infrastructure**; Offering's Capability's Resources, Activities and Partners & Suppliers
    (one "+" for the tile: it adds a partner). NOT on Completed (either), Founders, Workspace, Delivery or a channel tile.
    A 32px square (radius 8px, the x's size) at the tile's LOWER RIGHT, 12px from the edges; the list keeps clear above it (the layer's bottom padding grows by the box and 5px). The "+" is two 2px bars 16px long drawn from the
    middle of the box (`::before`, `::after` at `top/left: 50%`, `translate(-50%, -50%)`): no glyph of text, so its centre IS the box's (the bars' computed centres equal the box's to 0.01px; the ink, measured on a screenshot, is
    within 0.5px). **A swap of two colours** (150ms): at REST it is TRANSPARENT (a wired button: an outline and a symbol, no fill; it takes the colour of what it stands on, `--plus-ground`: the tile's, the card's) with a 2px stroke and the "+" in the darker tone of the hue (`--plus-fill`:
    36% saturation, 38% lightness; the lighter tone in dark); under the pointer or the focus it is FILLED with the darker tone, no stroke (the border turns transparent: the fill shows under it, nothing moves) and the "+" is the ground
    colour. No growth of its own: the tile grows around it. The "+" against its ground is 5.88:1 (light), 6.43 (sepia), 5.28 (dark), the same in both states. Named `Add <Item> to <tile>` ("Add Activity to Operational", "Add Resource to Utilities").
    Pressing it adds a blank card of that tile's kind at the END of its list and opens it as a floating card, its Name focused; left completely blank it is removed again, as on first open. **On a resource group** it makes the
    resource in the group's own "Other" subsection -- the first one the founder added, or a new one, unnamed (it reads "Unnamed Subsection" until named, as the dialog's "Add Other" makes it) -- and a blank card left behind is removed together
    with the subsection the "+" made for it. The same box at the x's size (`.plus-box-small`, 32px, bars 14px) is the Activity card's Add Team (below). The small "+" glyph of an empty tile is gone from every tile that has the box.
- **A floating card's number cells at their width step** (owner, 2026-11-18 18:01: "Rearrange the fields and their cells ... what type of cells could be used according to their inputs"): every number cell of a floating card is the step its figure needs (`numberStep`, from the digits its limits allow: a percent, hours, weeks, months or a count XS, a volume Code, money S), never stretched across its column, at its column's left (`demand.css`, one rule). A range stands on one row, each label beside its cell (a competitor's From and To, the Code step).
- **A field on one row, its cell at its step** (owner, 2026-11-19 13:34 UTC+2: "I have said it already; check the input and drop-down cells to ensure they are the proper size. If it is possible, make the fields align horizontally, to save space"; 13:45, of the Infrastructure Partners & Suppliers card: "This is the proper layout for the floated cards"): a field's label -- a side label or a tick and its words -- its cell and its unit stand on ONE row wherever the card holds them: the label in the card's label column (`--card-label-col`), the cell after it at its step (`numberStep`: money S, a volume Code, a percent, hours or months XS; a dropdown as wide as its longest option), the unit after the cell ("☑ Allowance [cell] GBP a month"; Price, its currency and its Frequency on one row). An extra -- an Allowance, a Commission, a Bonus, a space's charge -- takes up to 9,999,999.99 (`NUMBER_LIMITS.moneyExtra`), so its cell is the Code step and the tick, the cell and the unit hold one row of a 492px card; the "Hours a month they can give" of a Partner, Supplier or Staff card is a side-label row, its cell XS. A field goes under its label only where the card is too narrow for the row. The founder's Working Hours and Delivery Hours stay side by side (rule 6 of "Floating cards"). The probe fails a number cell wider than its step and a field under its label that would fit on its row.
- **A floating card's groups are boxes of the shared bands** (owner, 2026-11-18 10:38): a card that asks its questions in groups draws each group as Customer Engagement's do -- `SingleColumn`, `EngagementBoxes`, `EngagementBox`: a title band (light capitals on the box tint, the 4px bar at the left), then its questions in bands, the tints alternating, the double rule between boxes -- and stands straight in the floating card's `column-tile-content`, so its bands run to the card's edges. Customer Engagement's cards, the Delivery card (Capacity & Delivery) and the Revenue Stream card follow it.
- **A floating card of questions (the Profile card is the model; the next card takes this)**: The questions are an engine file (`ProfileQuestions.ts`, as `SegmentQuestions.ts`: id, box, label, options, help, `profileQuestionAsked`, starting values, `profileSettle`); the card draws them with the existing parts only (`FieldRow`, `RadioChoice` or `FittingRadioChoice`, `BusinessTicks`, `NumberCell`, `OptionalNote`, `NumberedListInput`, `BusinessGroup`). Nothing is drawn for one card.
  - **Width and the content line.** The card is `--open-card-width` (492px) like every floating card, and its edge, ground and shadow are in the hue of its Segment or card (a card inside the tile's wrapper too). Content (name cell, options, notes, lists, cells) stops at ONE line: the right edge of the name cell, before the column of the x. Bands, their tints, the lines under labels and the rules run on from side to side of the card (1px from each side); the x stands beside the name cell, never in it.
  - **Three levels, one design each.** SECTIONS (Description, Motivations, Needs): a band on the primary fill, light capitals, 18px regular, as the Segment card's title bands (heading level 3), split from each other by the SHARED double rule (`business-capability-card-rule-double`: 4px, two 1px lines; never a variant), which the section heading draws itself (`BusinessGroup`, owner, 2026-11-11): no card places it, and the first section, under the card's head, has none; the layout probe fails a section after the first without it. SUBSECTIONS (the boxes): lighter, a 24% tint of the primary fill (owner, 2026-11-10; 2026-11-19 reconciliation), a 4px primary bar at the left, 14px bold capitals spaced wide in the title ink (level 4); one 1px line between them. QUESTIONS: the label over its options (18px regular, sentence case, "i" after the last word), a line under the label only where it stands over options; every other band is tinted. A section with no subsections (Needs) puts its labelled bands straight under its band.
  - **The Profile card is the pattern for every card, its titles only where they are defined** (owner, 2026-11-19 21:44 UTC+2: "Check the Profile card's style (sections, subsections), and use it as a pattern for the others"; 22:48: "Titles - only when they have been defined, or already exist."). Checked on 2026-11-20, card by card, as drawn: the head (number, name, x); then SECTIONS, each a run of bands, its title band on the dark fill only where the section has a defined name; inside them SUBSECTIONS, a caps title band each where they have one; the questions in alternating bands; Notes the last band. The Competitor and Offering cards follow it as the Profile does (DESCRIPTION, then their boxes). The Infrastructure cards (Resource, Activity, Partner, Provider) follow it with untitled sections (`BandSection`: dates, type, status, costs, options, team, notes, links; owner, 2026-11-18 22:00: "I did not ask for titles") and the one titled section that is defined, OFFERINGS, drawn only where it has content; their questions keep their side labels (section 8, "A field on one row", owner 2026-11-19 13:34-13:45). A title stands only where it was defined or already exists: `EngagementBands` (a section with no question draws nothing), `DialogSectionBand` and `titledLines` (a tile's sub-title) draw no title over nothing (`tileParts.test.tsx`). A new card takes the same parts; it names a section only where the owner or the design has named it.
  - **One band pattern on every card and dialog** (Profile, Competitor, Offering, the Value Proposition and the Competitors' dialog; 2026-11-09; the Organisation, Founders & Ownership (and a founder's own card) and Registrations dialogs the Legal requirement and expense cards and the Brand's colour, typeface and image cards the same evening (their fields on the card's ground, their Notes the next band, tinted); the free-text dialogs (Capability, Purpose, the Brand's texts) stay as they are, and so do the Strategy cards (owner, 2026-11-09), the first of the Identity System's, owner: "one style for all cards in the Foundation capability"). Under any title band (a section's or a box's) the first band is untinted, then the bands alternate with the 12% tint (`legal-entity-org-band`; owner, 2026-11-10; 2026-11-19 reconciliation), and the Notes are the next tint in the run. **One question per band** (but Infrastructure & Capability's cards, which band by section: section 8, "Infrastructure & Capability's cards: one band a section"): every question is a band of its own, never a second row in another's band (owner, 2026-11-09: the Offering's O3b), so the tints alternate question by question; `npm run check:card` and the layout probe fail a band that holds two labelled field rows. The 24% tint with its bar is for box titles only: a question is never put first in a `business-segment-box` (its first band is styled as the box's title); a question standing alone under a section goes straight in the `legal-entity-field`. `npm run check:card` compares the colours drawn (`getComputedStyle().backgroundColor`), not the classes. **The card's end takes its last band's colour** (`cardEndGround.ts`): every kind of band is named once, in `CARD_BANDS` (an Organisation section, a Legal or Expense card's row, a founder's band, a Brand inventory's row); a new kind of band is added there, and the layout probe ("a card ends in its last band's colour", measured from the colours drawn) fails on one it does not know. **Groups side by side** (the Value Proposition's Emotional and Social Needs, 2026-11-09) are one step of the run: a band each, in two columns, both the same tint, as tall as the taller; where they stand one under the other the run goes on band by band (section 10, "The Value Proposition dialog reads in three steps").
  - **The card's end is its last band's colour** (owner, 2026-11-09: "The end of all the cards' colour should follow the last field's colour"): below the last band, the card's padding and any room a dialog keeps under its content take that band's colour, so the run of tints ends with the card (`cardEndGround.ts`, on every dialog and floating card; `npm run check:card` fails a card whose end shows another colour).
  - **Divider lines** (owner, 2026-11-09). A horizontal line runs from side to side of its card or dialog (a dialog's scrolling body reaches the dialog's sides, so nothing clips it). A vertical line runs down to the horizontal line under its columns, or to the end of its section; where its band's colour runs on to the card's end, the line runs on with it (`cardEndGround.ts`).
  - **A titled section of a dialog under what stands above it** (the Segment Description's Profiles, the Competitors' Differentiation, the Founders & Ownership's founder Profiles): the shared double rule, a title band on the primary fill, its content in a band under it (`DialogSectionBand`).
  - **A band has room** (owner, 2026-11-10: "Give the proper space to the coloured bands, so to be recognised as a field. - General rule!"): 12px above and below its content on every card and dialog, so each band reads as a field of its own; set once in the shared band styles, never per card.
  - **A light line between two bands** (owner, 2026-11-10, a general rule): where one band follows another, a 1px line in a light tint of the card's colour (owner: "I did not say white, but a light card's colour tint!"): 30% of the card's ink in its light tone; in the dark, the card's ink (`--band-divider`), stands between them, each band keeping its 12px (12px, 1px, 12px). Inside ONE page-3 section band no line stands between its questions, but between two questions that are both a tick box or a radio choice (owner, 2026-11-18 23:56 UTC+2).
  - **The light line is only between coloured bands** (owner, 2026-11-10: "The divide lines should be light hue only between the colour transit parts. Not the vertical, double or the under the labels one"): `--band-divider` stands where one band's colour gives way to the next (the Workbook's rows too); a column's vertical divider, the double rule, the rule under a column's title, a list's row lines keep their own grey.
  - **A card that ends with a band ends with the band's own 12px**: the card's padding under it goes, so the band runs to the edge (it stood 24px under the last field); the head of a card stands on the ground and its first band is tinted (a founder's Roles as a Profile's first band); the end of the card is painted on whole pixels (a part-pixel drew a darker line under the last band).
  - **One colour for every card's bands** (owner, 2026-11-10, a general rule): the channel card's saturation and lightness in the card's own hue: `--card-band-ink` for a section's or box's title band, `--tint-row-band` (12%) for the rows that alternate, `--tint-box-title` (24%) for a single box's title.
  - **Nested cards swap two tints** (owner, 2026-11-10: "In the nested cards, swap the same colour, and keep their wireframes"): a card inside a card takes the other of the two tints of its hue, every card keeping its 1px edge; never bands inside each card.
  - **Flush.** A band follows the one above with no gap, a section band follows its rule, and its first band follows it; the card's row gap is taken back where a rule or a band joins.
  - **Text holders and side cells.** A label over a text holder (a list of lines, a text box) has no line under it. A label with its cell beside it (the age, a square number cell) has no line either; the cell is at the right end of the band, equal room above and below, the label centred on it.
  - **Every part ends with the Note** (`OptionalNote`), a band of its own, the next tint in the run, a field gap above its tick row; what it holds is what the choices do not.
  - **Options.** Similar things are joined with a slash ("Laptop / desktop", "Non-profit / charity / NGO"), different things with the word "or" ("Owner or senior executive", "Invoice, bank transfer or purchase order") (owner, 2026-11-03); there is no "None of these" (an Other the user writes in); a limit shows beside the label ("3 max") and locks the rest without a warning; short radios stand in one row or one column.
  - **Accessibility.** Text 4.5:1 (a locked option 3:1), a control's boundary 3:1 on the card's ground (`--cell-edge`, a list's border; the cells' soft edge is below it by the owner's choice, 2026-11-09, and the card audit reports it, measured as drawn), a cell's fill a warm off-white (`--cell-fill`), targets 24px, every control named, headings that do not skip a level, help points of 160 characters at most.
  - **Examples.** The examples answer the card's questions (a test checks that each answer stands against its Segment) and a note holds only what the choices do not.
  - **The audit.** `npm run check:card` opens the card in sepia, light and dark at 1440 wide and in sepia at 520 wide, and checks all of the above on the page (`CARD_OPEN="..."` names the tile of the card; `BASE_URL` the running app). A new card passes it before it is done.
- **Floating cards** (`FloatingCards`). One card alone, or a parent and its child stacked (below), in a portal over the page, which is neither darkened nor inert (section 10, "Floating card" and "A dialog darkens the page behind it, a floating card does not"): a `role="dialog"` `aria-modal`, each card in an opaque ground (the page card's own, in the hue
  of the card the tile stands in) with a **drop shadow** (`0 24px 56px --shadow-18`, `0 8px 20px --shadow-12`), and no Back button: it closes by a press outside it or Escape (section 10, "Floating card"). **The chapter behind the card -- the frame it stands over -- is shaded and frozen** by `.floating-chapter-shade` (the frame's size and corners, the shadow tone over the chapter, see-through, never blurred: "The shade behind a floating card is a shadow", section 11; a press on it closes the card). **The rest of the page is not darkened** (`--floating-scrim` is clear: section 10, "A dialog darkens the page behind it, a floating card does not"; section 11, "Shaded once, and only where nothing shades it already") -- no blur, no lifted
  brightness, no inset light or shade on the page behind (those are the grouped boxes' own); the dialog keeps its own solid ground ("One ground for every dialog and its cards", below). Escape and a click outside the card close (no Back; 2026-11-19 reconciliation)
  it (an open picker or "i" takes its own Escape first); the focus returns to the control that opened it (the page is never inert; 2026-11-19 reconciliation: after the render; quietly after a click, see the reaction). **The box name above each card:** a centred caption, 12px over the
  card, outside it -- the name of the tile or box it belongs to ("Operational", "Providers", "Founders", "Workspace"; a child's: its tile's) -- as plain title text (`--font-h3`, bold, `--floating-caption-ink`, white) on the backdrop, not in a cell, box or
  input, **with the box's "i" after it** where the box has a note (the same note its dialog's header carries; the Workspace's, which has no dialog header, is only here). Contrast of the name on the scrim, over the worst pixel of the page behind it
  (measured with the text hidden): light 6.26:1, sepia 6.31, dark 19.1.
  Width: every card `--open-card-width` (492px, at most the window less 16px), a founder's too (owner, 2026-11-19 01:41 UTC+2). **Placed on the first row:** the TOP of the first card stands on the top of the first row of tiles of its
  page column as the page rests (scrolled up) -- that row's top now plus how far the column's body is scrolled, so the same y however the page was scrolled -- and the box name stands above that line (over the column's first heading), on every page alike (owner, 2026-11-19 13:45 UTC+2, of the Infrastructure Partners & Suppliers card standing so: "This is the proper layout for the floated cards"; this retires the 2026-11-14 23:25 point that moved a card down to keep its name clear of a chapter's title, which set the Brand, Legal Entity, Engagement, financial and Foundation cards 52 to 56px lower than Infrastructure's); horizontally it is centred over the frame on the half of the page its tile is not in (owner, 2026-11-12 17:04; 2026-11-19 reconciliation; "Floating cards", rule 2), within the
  card of the page it belongs to (Infrastructure's or Offering's Capability's rectangle; the Workspace over the tile group of 1. Resources). A taller stack extends downward and, once it would pass the footer, scrolls inside itself (the captions and the cards
  scroll); it is kept 8px inside the window. Where that first row is out of view -- the cards stacked, under a 700px page card, and the page scrolled -- the stack stands at the top of the band instead (8px under the page heading's underline, never lower than 200px above the footer). jumpTo / Review / search / the Financial links to the Workspace press its tile, which opens the form alone.
  **In a Segment's chapter, kept on the first row** (owner, 2026-11-19 23:48 UTC+2, "Keep it as it is"): a floating card stays on the first row of tiles, its box name over the chapter's title, which the scrim dims. The chapter frame's top was weighed and set aside: the name (about 57px) cannot stand between the page title's underline and the frame's top (13px) without covering the page title, which stays in place and uncovered (owner, 2026-11-11, "the page title, should always stay in place"). Dialogs open at the frame's top (section 23).
- **Every floating card starts at one level**: the first row of tiles of its column where that stands under the column's title, else the frame's top plus the title's height (62px); a card opened by a link (a Legal Entity pair) stands over the other column of the tile that holds it, at the same level.
- **The caption is the section's name** however the card is opened (its tile, its group's tile, another card's link): "Resources", "Activities", "Partners & Suppliers", "Founders". Never a group's name; the opener passes no name of its own.
- **A card opens on the half of the page its tile is not in** (`otherColumn`, `acrossFrom`): over the frame on the other half; a card opened from a tile inside a dialog (a category's, the Inventory's, the Completed's, a channel's), however it is opened (pressed, or asked for by a line or a link), goes by that tile's place. **A page of one chapter** (owner, 2026-11-12 17:04: "the floated cards should appear at the other side (next chapter)"): a card opened from its dialog stands in the empty half beside the chapter, the place a second chapter would take, where the window has room for it (`emptyHalfCentre`); where it has none (the chapters stack), over the chapter. The layout probe checks it: "a floating card opened from a dialog stands on the other half of the page, clear of the dialog's tiles".
- **Every floating card shows its record time under it** ("Recorded 17/10/2026 14:13", `CardRecordService`): set the first time the card is made or opened, kept in the organisation's document (`cardTimes`). The record tag stands 12px under the card, as the title tag stands 12px over it; both tags are drawn as the card is (its ground, ink, corners, raised edge and shadow) with 6px above and below their words and 12px at the sides.
- **A status and the activity it chooses: PARENT -> CHILD.** A resource (or the Workspace) whose Status is "Activity" chooses ONE activity on its **Activity** row (its `activityId`;
  `activityRows`, `capability/useCapabilityState.tsx`): a picker of the activities of its scope (one that ended before the card starts is not offered) and "+ Add", which makes a new activity
  (`newActivityFor`, its `madeBy` the card's id); an earlier answer's activity made by a status (named by its `sourceId`) is read as the one the card chooses (`AnswerBusinessCapability.ts`).
  - an activity the card made is named on the card ("Name the activity", under the picker); on the activity's own card the name stands where the cell was, a **link** to the parent card
    (`.business-capability-child-name`: `--text-primary`, underlined and bolder under the pointer and the focus);
  - beside the picker the chosen activity's link (`LinkSymbol`, "Open Activity “<name>”") opens the pair;
  - a link either way opens BOTH as floating cards, stacked: the **parent above the child**, 12px apart (each with its own box name above it, the caption of the child 12px over it), on one centre line, the parent's top on the first row's top; whatever was open gives way; Back or
    Escape closes both; if either is gone (the Status changed in the open parent) the pair closes.
  The Workspace is the parent of the same tie but its form is FROZEN: it gets no link line (its own line "Recorded under 2. Activities as ..." stays as it is, in the form); its child links back to it.
- **Tile grids: ONE SHAPE.** (A page's master tiles keep one size and the 2 x 2 for three or four: section 23, "Master Tiles never change their sizes, and layout".) Every set of tiles, subcategory boxes and fixed card boxes takes the same shape
  (`gridColumns(n)`, mirrored by the probe): **one to three in a single row, exactly four as a 2 x 2 square, five or more
  in TWO rows** (`ceil(n / 2)` across, the last row possibly shorter). On the page the groups are left-aligned, 12px apart every way,
  the tiles square and never stretched, a group too narrow for its row wrapping to fewer across: Infrastructure ›
  1. Resources: six tiles, 3 x 2 (Founders, Workspace, Utilities, Workstation, Equipment, Digital Infrastructure), with no
  "Organisational / Operational Resources" headings and no rule down the left; 2. Activities: Operational, Production,
  Marketing, Sales & Customer as a **2 x 2 square** (a category the founder added earlier is one more tile while it holds
  activities: five tiles are 3 x 2, six 3 x 2, seven 4 x 2 ...) (the grey **Completed** tile does not stand among them: it stands in the footer band, see section 12); 3. Partners &
  Suppliers: Partners, Providers (one row of two). Offering's Capability: Resources, Activities,
  Partners & Suppliers, Delivery as a 2 x 2 square; with channel tiles 5 or 6 tiles are 3 x 2 and 7 or more two rows with more
  across (4 x 2, 5 x 2 ...; each channel tile after its category). Where the old rule ("up to 3 x 3") gave another shape:
  the Activities group with five categories or more was a column of two across (three rows) and is now two rows; Offering's
  Capability with seven or more tiles was 3 x 3 and is now 4 x 2 or wider; Offering's two partner kinds in a dialog were a column
  of two and are now a row of two.
  **A list of cards one adds to is three in a row**: a dialog's cards with their "+ Add card" (`CardBoxes` with an `add`: Additional Legal Requirements, Expenses, a category's cards ...) stand three across however many there are (`CARD_LIST_COLUMNS`), and the add box takes the next free place, after the last card (a fourth card's row has the add box beside it); fewer across only where the dialog is too narrow for three, and **two a row in the vertical layout, one on a phone** (section 23; owner, 2026-11-22 09:58 UTC+2). **A dialog's list of cards with no add box is three in a row too** (owner, 2026-11-18 00:46: "Place 3 tiles per row, like the other dialogue cards"): Sales Forecast's Customer Sources, a relationship type's cards, each Offering's Delivery card, the Completed activities (`CardBoxes`, its grid marked `.card-list`). The probe checks all three. The page's groups of tiles keep the one shape.
  **There is no tail tile.** The grey Completed tile is not part of any group's grid: it is one of the two rectangles of the Inventory and Completed row, in the footer band level with Back (section 12), and the same tile on every page. Infrastructure's three sections keep their heading (number, title, "i") as a PLAIN heading of
  `--section-head-height` (36px) over their tiles: **no fold arrow and no fold behaviour, and no "Expand all"** on them (the
  tiles are the grouping). Offering's Capability holds its tiles directly, under a heading-height **spacer**
  (`.business-capability-heading-spacer`, `aria-hidden`, the same 36px and 12px as Infrastructure's first heading, no text: the
  column title is the card's label), so the first tile row of both cards starts at one y.
- **The two cards.** 1. Infrastructure and 2. Offering's Capability stand
  **side by side**, each in one of the page's two equal columns
  (`--space-column`, 12px, between them), tops level, each as tall as its
  content; each card takes the page's 13px side padding on its outer side only,
  so they are equally wide and never overlap. Only when the page (`page-card`
  container) is under 700px wide (as on every page of chapters) are they stacked, Infrastructure above
  Capability, each the page's full width (nothing then to line up across them).
  **The column title is the card's one label and its first row** -- "1.
  INFRASTRUCTURE", "2. OFFERING'S CAPABILITY" (the `answer-column-label` caps
  title, `--font-h3`, centred) -- inside the card: the card has **no head row of
  its own** (no fold arrow, no second "N. Name" label, no "i"; the page's
  guidance says what the old "i" said). Vertically, inside a card: padding 12px,
  the title row, 12px to the first section heading (Infrastructure) or the
  heading-height spacer (Capability), 12px from a heading to its tiles, 12px from
  the tiles to the next heading, 12px to the card's end -- all box gaps, a
  section heading (36px) being the shared height. Measured at 1600px: the title
  row 12px under the card's top edge, its first tile row 277px from the page top
  in both cards, tiles 214.7px, gaps 12px.
- **The label arrow and its drop-down help.** This page has NO "Hide Help" /
  "Need Help?" button (`Conversation.tsx` passes the page's `titleHelpArrows`
  condition; the other pages keep theirs). Each card title has a **label arrow**
  (`FoldArrow.tsx`, `label-arrow.css`, drawn by `CardTitleHelp.tsx`) after its text
  and its "i", 8px from the previous item of the title row, centred on
  the title's line: pointing right while the title's help is hidden (the default),
  down while open. It is NOT the 36px fold arrow: it is the label arrow (see "The
  label arrow"), a triangle the title's size (`--label-arrow-size: var(--font-h2)`),
  its hit area never under 24px (28px at this title), `aria-expanded`, `aria-controls` and the
  name "Show help for <title>" / "Hide help for <title>"; keyboard operable;
  an outline at rest, filled under the pointer and the keyboard focus (no ground, no ring). The help is a **drop-down
  panel** (`CardTitleHelp.tsx`): absolutely positioned under the title row
  (6px, `--space-label`), the card content's width, the help box's ground
  (`--bg-help`, no stroke) with a shadow, `z-index: 20` above the tiles -- it
  takes no room, so nothing below it moves. One panel is open at a time (opening
  the other title's closes it); it closes with its arrow, **Escape** (the key is
  consumed, so it is not the page's Back; the focus returns to the arrow) or a
  press anywhere outside the title and the panel. The open state is view-only
  (never saved; there is no saved help state on any page: the old "Hide Help"
  was `useState(true)` in `Conversation.tsx`, also unsaved).
- **Channel tiles.** A card a Customer Channel made ("+ Add expenses" on a
  channel: a Resource in Offering's Capability › Resources linked by
  `engagement.channelResources`; a partner or provider so linked) is NOT
  among the user's own cards: it has its own tile in its category, next to
  that category's tile, in a hue of its own (`CHANNEL_TILE_HUE`, the
  palette's teal, apart from the Infrastructure rose and the Capability
  terracotta; the tile, its dialog and the cards inside take it through
  `--strategy-theme-hue`): "Channel resources" after Resources, "Channel
  partners" after Partners & Suppliers. A tile is drawn only when the
  category has such cards (never empty); it has no "+ Add". Inside, the cards
  are the same cards (same editor, same columns) and each keeps its
  "For channel: ..." line with the way back to its channel. A card of
  Infrastructure linked from a channel stays in Infrastructure.
- **Dialog: ONE frame, the page's width, between the page heading's underline and the
  footer.** `role="dialog"`, `aria-modal`, named by the section (the tile's title,
  `aria-labelledby`; "Utilities, Electricity" while a subcategory is shown), over a dimmed
  overlay, the page behind inert. EVERY dialog (Founders, Workspace, Utilities, Workstation,
  Equipment, Digital Infrastructure, Activities, Partners & Suppliers, the Capability
  sections, the channel tiles) has the same frame: its vertical extent is the space between
  the **underline of the page heading** (`.interview-divider`, never above the fixed app
  header) and the **footer** (`.workspace-footer`, the fixed 64px band at the bottom of the
  window that holds "← Back"; its top edge), and it is as wide as the page's **card area**
  (`.conversation-card`, the width the two page cards share: 1400px at 1600, 1254px at
  1366, at most 92vw), centred across the window. There is no square dialog and no wide
  exception any more (the tiles and the subcategory/founder boxes stay true squares). It is measured when it opens (`dialogFrame()`,
  `SectionTiles.tsx`) so the text boxes inside lay out at their final width, and again when the
  window is resized; without those lines (a test, a page without the heading) the CSS
  fallback is the least of 92vw and 1400px wide, 88vh high, centred. It scrolls inside its
  body only, when the content is taller. It holds the section's own content, unchanged: its
  heading and "i" notes, "Expand all", its cards fully editable, the same folds. The
  **Back** button ("← Back") stands at the lower left, always in view below the scrolling
  body; **Escape** does the same and nothing else (the key is consumed, so it is not the
  page's Back; an open list or "i" inside takes its own Escape first, and an inner view -- a
  subcategory, an open founder box -- is left before the dialog is closed); a **click on the
  dimmed backdrop** (outside the dialog, on an empty space) closes the whole dialog -- a
  press that starts inside the dialog (a text drag) and ends outside never does, a click
  inside never does. One dialog at a time. Opening is view-only state, never saved.
- **One ground for every dialog and its cards (solid).** The dialog's ground is its
  card's own (the hue of the card it opens from: Infrastructure rose, Capability terracotta,
  channel teal -- the only thing that differs), SOLID: no transparency, no blur, no `backdrop-filter`, so nothing of the page shows through (a frame that has text on it
  is never transparent). The card's edge colour, `box-shadow: 0 16px 40px`, padding `--card-padding`.
  The cards inside any dialog (`.section-tile-dialog .business-foundation-card`) are solid too: the inner tint, the same edge -- one rule, not per-dialog colours.
  **Contrast:** text on a solid ground is held at 4.5:1 or more by the colour tokens of each theme (the check reads it).
- **The dialog header.** The section's heading is the dialog's title (title style, `--font-h3`, 24px; every dialog alike, one drawn in a field page's wrapper too -- Founders & Ownership and the Mission & Vision pair stood at 18px until 2026-11-20); the views opened inside the
  dialog follow it ON THE SAME LINE after a slash -- "6. Digital Infrastructure / Software / Xero accounting", "Activities / Completed /
  Finished one", "1. Founders / Maya Chen" (`DialogHeading`, fed by `useDialogInnerBack`): the slash 8px each side after the root title and
  6px each side inside the trail of views, the names the body size (18; a size under the root title), regular weight, `--text-muted`, one line high; a long header cuts the names with an ellipsis (the full name is the tooltip) and keeps
  the "i" after them. The "i" is the nearest note along the way (the subcategory's, else the title's). There is no separate subtitle row: a
  fixed subcategory has no head row of its own (an added "Other" subcategory keeps its name box and its x). The accessible name is
  "Title, Subtitle, ..." (the names joined by commas).
- **Content centred, at the top.** What a dialog holds -- a grid of tiles or boxes (its boxes left-aligned inside their own block, the block
  centred), the opened card, the Workspace card, a founder's cards, an empty message -- is **centred across the dialog and starts at its top**
  (12px under the title row), never in the middle of its height; what is taller scrolls in the body. The title row keeps its place at the
  dialog's left.
- **Subcategory boxes.** A section with MORE THAN ONE subcategory opens, inside its
  dialog, on a grid of SQUARE BOXES, one per subcategory (`SubcategoryBoxes`, the
  section tile component: the same title, underline and list, colour, the "&" rule), in the **one shape** (the add box counts as
  one), the side = (content width - gaps) / columns, **never more than a third of the content width and never so tall that its rows do not
  fit the dialog** (`(dialog height - 160px - (rows - 1) * 12px) / rows`). Measured with Utilities (5 boxes + add = 6, 3 x 2): 301px squares at 1600 x 1000
  (content 1375px), 201px at 1366 x 800; Workstation, Equipment, Digital Infrastructure (3 + add = 4, 2 x 2) the same size; Offering's two
  partner kinds (no add box) are one row of two. They are: Utilities (Electricity, Internet, Heating & Gas, Cooling & Air Conditioning, Water),
  Workstation (Furniture, Electrical, Supporting), Equipment (Computing, Peripherals, Specialised), Digital Infrastructure (Software, Digital
  Services, Online Presence & Communication), each plus any "Other" subsection the founder added (named "Unnamed Subsection" until named in its
  head), and, in Offering's Capability, Partners & Suppliers (Partners, Providers; the Channel partners tile shows
  the kinds that have some, one kind alone directly). A box lists its cards' names (or shows the "+" while it has none). "Cooling & Air Conditioning" is drawn on two lines in its box too (a forced break after "Air": "Cooling & Air" / "Conditioning"); the stored id ("cooling", "heating") and every saved answer are unchanged -- nothing is stored by label. Pressing a box shows that
  subcategory's card boxes in the same dialog in place of the grid (the header names it after a slash); **Back and Escape return to the box grid
  first, then close the dialog** (`useDialogInnerBack`, one level per nested view); a backdrop click closes the whole dialog. A section with one
  subcategory (an Activities category, Offering's Resources, Delivery, a single partner kind) shows its content directly. **The add box.** Where a
  founder can add a subcategory of their own (the four resource groups: "Other") the grid ends with a HALF-SIZE BOX after the last box: a true
  square of half their side, a dashed, lighter edge and a plain ground to read as an action, a "+" over "Add Other" centred, at the TOP LEFT of
  its grid cell; `aria-label="Add Other"`, keyboard-operable like a tile. Pressing it adds an Other subcategory and opens it at once, to be named
  in its head. The grid is reset each time the dialog opens. Links: a box carries `data-anchor` ("subsection-<group>-<kind>"), `data-holds` (its
  cards' ids) and `data-opens`; `jumpTo.ts` presses the tile (`data-tile` + `data-holds`), then the subcategory box, then the card's box.
- **Card tiles** (`CardTile.tsx`, `card-tile.css`; `CardBoxes.tsx` lays them out). ONE tile is a card wherever cards are shown as tiles: every dialog's list of cards
  (Resources, Activities, Partners & Suppliers, a category's / subcategory's, the channel tiles', the Completed activities) and the Inventory overlay. It is
  the page tile's make (`TileFace`: the name with its rule, then a list of lines), 215px (the page tile's side, read from a page tile), in the colour of the
  section it stands in (Infrastructure, Offering's Capability, a Customer Channel's teal; a legal expense the Legal Entity's neutral ground). Under the name
  (with its number where the list is numbered: "1. Xero accounting") the lines are the same everywhere: where it belongs (section, group, subgroup), its
  status, its cost and frequency. A small coloured circle per link (Activity, Investigate, Partner, Provider), at the tile's lower left; **the list keeps clear of the circles** (it ends 4px above them, as a tile's list keeps clear of its "+"; the owner's bug report of 2026-11-19: the circles stood on the list's last line). Hover is the page tile's (lift, glow,
  transition) at 110%, growing INWARDS from the side of the grid it stands on. Grids start at the left, in the ONE SHAPE of every tile grid ("Tile grids", above: `CardBoxes` gives `TileGrid` three across for every dialog's card list, with its add box or without (`CARD_LIST_COLUMNS`; owner, 2026-11-09 and 2026-11-18 00:46), and a page's own groups of tiles keep the `gridColumns` of their cards; fewer across only where the dialog is too narrow; the Strategy cards' `threeAcross` grids keep their three), 12px apart; the add box ends
  the flow, in the next free place, (half the side, dashed, "Add Resource" / "Add Activity" / "Add Partner" / "Add Provider"). A tile is pressed to open its card FLOATING; Activities
  stand in the order of their start dates, then their names, and are never dragged (2026-11-22; rule | was | now: no drag without a one-press way, WCAG 2.5.7 | ordered by dragging one onto another | by date, nothing to move); a completed activity's tile is the grey.
  A section with no cards opens directly on ONE new blank card, as before. There is no other tile markup or stylesheet for cards: change `CardTile` /
  `card-tile.css` and every grid changes. (`cardBoxLayout` and the 96px floor are gone; the probe's card tile rule checks side, gaps, title size, add box.)
- **Completed activities (the grey).** The CARDS of the finished ones are grey too, wherever they are opened -- the card boxes, the Completed tile's floating cards, the Completed dialog's category boxes: the Completed
  tint for the ground and edge (`.business-capability-activity-completed`), the muted grey for the text (7.17:1 at the least in the light theme). Infrastructure › 2. Activities has a grey **Completed** tile (`SectionTile.completed`, the neutral
  `--bg-completed-card` ground, `--line-completed` edge, `--text-completed-ink` text: 9.78:1 light, 6.28 sepia, 11.07 dark; no category hue; its
  title the same grey, its dialog grey too) in the Inventory and Completed row: a rectangle with its title only, about 56px high, standing in the footer band level with Back, taking no height of its own and one size on every page (section 12). It holds every activity
  ticked Completed, **each stored where it always was, in its category's list** (the flag only moves where it is shown: no migration); a
  category's tile and dialog show the active ones only (its "Active N" counts them); ticking Completed on a card files it under Completed,
  un-ticking returns it to its category. Up to 8 completed activities are card boxes in one flat grid (all inactive, numbered 1..n, no add box:
  an activity becomes completed by ticking, not by adding); more than 8 first show BOXES named after the categories that hold finished ones
  (the four defaults as a 2 x 2 square, plus custom ones), each opening that category's finished ones. The tile exists only while an activity is completed (no
  "+"); un-ticking the last one closes the dialog by itself. **Offering's Capability has the same grey Completed TILE** (the one tile of the row, not one per section: it holds the completed activities of both cards; no "+" box; the same look, links to frozen cards, dialog of grey card boxes; present only while one is completed); it replaces the nested "Completed" box the Activities dialog used to end with (`CardBoxes folder`, removed), and a completed Offering activity leaves the Activities tile's list. Its floating card stays open when the tick is taken off (the activity goes back to the Activities tile).
  **A completed activity is FROZEN** wherever it is opened (both sections): every field of its card sits in a disabled `fieldset.business-capability-frozen` (`display: contents`: it takes no box) -- the name, the performers and their "+" / x, the Responsible and its tick, the Description and its tick, the linked Offerings, the x -- and only the **Completed tick** is outside it, live and normal, so it can be unticked (which unfreezes the card and files it back in its category). The frozen look is rule 14 of section 6 (an ordinary card in four greys, `cursor: default`, no hover reaction: the hover rules all exclude `:disabled`). (A child activity's name link to its parent stays live: it is a way to another card, not a field.)
- **A completed card's tile is the completed grey, wherever it stands**: in the Inventory too (`inactive` on the card tile), as the Completed tile's cards and the completed floating card are.
- **The opened card** (a card box pressed, or a floating card; Resource of Infrastructure or Offering's Capability, Activity, Partner, Provider, channel-origin card).
  Centred, `--open-card-width` (**492px**, at most the window) wide, 12px padding (a 466px content width), label gap 6px, field gap 12px. **The head:** [name cell, its order number inside it] [x]: the arrow that once stood before the number is
  gone and the number is drawn over the left of the name cell, 10px in, the name's text after it (section 11, "Order number inside the name cell"; the marker column is 0); the number and the x are boxes as high as
  the one-line name cell (`--card-name-line`, 44.3px), centred in it, so they stay centred on the name's FIRST line and do not move when the name wraps: the cell grows downward only (measured with a 110-character name,
  one line 44.3px to three lines 98.3px: the number's and the x's centres at 34.8px both times, the first line's at 34.9px). **Nothing is stretched**: a picker (Status, Team, Responsible, Provider / Partner, Frequency) is as
  wide as its longest OPTION and no wider (the placeholder, "Select", is shorter than any option and is always shown whole): the Resource's Status 316px (its longest option 282.3px + 33.3px of padding, chevron and border), Frequency 127px ("Per project" 93.1px
  + 33.3px), measured to within 0.6px; the cost row is `[Qty] [Price] [currency] ... [Frequency]` (see "The cost row" below). **The side-label
  row** of a Resource -- the Status -- is a label (the "i" glued to it) beside the dropdown, mid-aligned, `--side-label-gap` (12px) apart, in the label column (`--side-label-short`, 177px), so the Status and the Responsible
  dropdowns start at one x (the currency stands over it).
