Two tints only (the Theme's and the Objective's), swapped from card to card; the x and the "i" have no fill at rest.
- **The Strategy System is one page** ("4. Strategy System", no page number; its rail names it): its frame is headed "Situation & Direction", centred. The Strategic Period's Goals, Objectives, Initiatives and Metrics are written in each Theme's card; the old pages 2 (Goals & Objectives) and 3 (Roadmap) are hidden sections (`HIDDEN_SECTIONS`, PageGroups.ts): they keep the saved answer but no page, bullet or arrow shows them.
- **The Strategic Period's tile** has a "+" (a Theme made and opened as a card with all its sections) and a hairline after its dates. Its dialog: the Start and the End side by side from the left edge, the Financial Plan in one line under them, then the tiles (the Roadmap, each Theme, and an "Add Card" tile of their size, which keeps the suggested Themes' menu). A Metric tile says the Objective's period, then its Baseline and Target.
- A Theme's card stands in the centre under its section's title.
- **The Strategic Period dialog's tiles and top row end where the Themes' tiles end**, at any width.
- **Founder's card in a page frame (labels beside cells)**: the four cells (Salary, Frequency, Working Hours, Delivery Hours) are 108px in two pairs; each label column is as wide as its widest label (at least 8.8 body-em, the Start date's column too), 12px before its cell, so no "i" runs under a cell.
- **A founder's floating card keeps its pairs side by side** (Salary | Frequency, Working Hours | Delivery Hours) where its fields have the room; where they are narrower than 300px (a small window, a card in a half-page column), the pairs stand one under the other, in one column, each label with its cell (owner, 2026-11-21 12:20 UTC+2, "As it is now": the app's `cost-fields` container query; it replaced "the label stands over its cell"). The funnel ends where the Start date's calendar box ends (container query on `cost-fields`).

## 14. Financial pages and Global Settings

New fields follow the rules above and reuse their neighbours' shapes; nothing here is a new pattern of its own.
- **Costs table (the Financials' Costs)**: one more column, **Cost nature**, after When, with an i in its header (`business-foundation-label-info`) and an `OptionPicker` (hug) in the row, plus a muted line for the part before the opening (the Financial sections will change). The Amount column (header and cells) is right-aligned and holds the figures alone; the currency is written once, in its header ("Amount (EUR)"). The grid's template in `conversation.css` has the extra column; the row's remove cell stays last.
- **Opening Date**: a date field on the Organisation tile (right column, after the Registration Date; design Questions 2, 8.5). The Global Settings show it as a record under Business stages, in the Plan Start's row style (label, value, no new row height); the Financial Foundation no longer has it.
- **Funds** (`data-anchor="financial-funds"`, under the Funding Need, in the Funding tile's Uses): the same cards as the funding-need cards (`.financial-need-card`) in the same grid; the Total Pre-operational Budget is a `.financial-total` line under the costs (`data-anchor="financial-pre-operational-budget"`). Without an Opening Date the block is replaced by one muted sentence.
- **Global Settings** (`/settings`, `pages/GlobalSettings.tsx`, `styles/global-settings.css`): a page of ordinary cards and fields on the existing tokens, opened from **Settings** in the header's Menu (`ToolsMenu`, `openSettings`); no new sizes.
- **Plan Start is never typed** (`derivedPlanStart`): the earlier of the Opening Date's month and the month of the earliest cost, a costed Business card's or a Legal Entity expense's (`legalExpenseDates`); the Global Settings show it as a read-only line. A cost dated before it counts as already paid, as the model has always had it. It is kept in the Financial Foundation answer too, so the documents read the same month.
- **The Opening Date** is the one date the user chooses (on the Organisation tile); a warning says when it is before the company was formed. The business stage follows it (design Questions 2, 8.2a); the Global Settings hold the stages' four durations in a table in the Assets' style.
- **VAT is answered once, on the Legal Entity.** Not registered there: Global Settings says "Not registered" and offers no choice. Registered: the one choice left is Amounts including or excluding the tax (`effectiveVatBasis`).
- **A "Per unit" frequency** is one option per unit of the group ("Per hour", "Per kg"); the plain "Per unit" is offered only where the group has no unit of its own.
- **Default useful life** (Global Settings > Assets): every asset group has a default, from `UsefulLifeReferences.ts` (IRS Publication 946 and the Texas DFPS asset schedule); an "i" beside the title says what useful life is, and one beside each group lists its 6 to 8 popular items with both schedules' lives and the sources as links.
- **Global Settings follows the Business style**: every detail is in an "i" beside its label; the Assets section is one table (capitalisation limit, each group's useful life, each asset's own years); Reset sets a group, or all of them, back to the default.
- Global Settings: figure boxes are 112px; the Assets table has four fixed columns (Asset, Cost, Useful life, Reset); the Capitalisation limit sits in Cost.
- Global Settings > Assets ends with "Intangible assets" (amortisation years by group, with FRS 102, IAS 38, US section 197 and common-practice sources).
- Global Settings: Intangible assets list only the groups that hold a card, each card numbered under its group with its own years.
- Global Settings > Channel Fee Table shows the Legal Entity country's rows (the UK's while none is chosen) that a subchannel's or an expense card's name takes, whatever their status (a provider not checked yet or not available there says so in its note), and every row the user made; (`shownChannelFees`). The rows appear by themselves from the user's channels, subchannels, expense cards and payment services (owner, 2026-11-15 16:37): no "+ Add channel" and no x; a row leaves with its channel. A paying subchannel the user named has its own row, "Entered by you" (`ownFeeChannelNames`); **Reset** under the table asks in the app's confirmation dialog (`.confirmation-overlay`) and restores the country's rows from the data, keeping the user's rows (`resetChannelFees`). The rest of the app's fee data stays in the table for when an answer names it (Principles, "Related to the answers").
- **Costs (4. Financials, the Financial Foundation chapter; Section 5, Costs, Funding & Position)**: three totals (Launch budget, Running costs a month, Cost of each sale), the Total Pre-operational Budget written as capital expenditure + startup + pre-operating, filters (group, Offering, nature, order), then one table under four headings, with "Not counted yet" last.
- **The Financials chapter** (built 2026-11-20): one chapter, Financial Foundation, in the 2 x 2; its Master Tiles and what each asks are Questions 20, section 14 (the one owner); the shared chapter frame and `SectionTiles` of every page (Situation & Direction's). Rule | was | now: a page asks, the documents record | five Master Tiles, Cash Flow and Break-Even & Sustainability asking nothing | three, the records in the Workbook's Financial Documents. The faces: a stream with no price says "Not priced yet" on Revenue Streams; **Funding** (`financial-funding`) says Funding Gap or Covered with the Runway, and the Total Funding Need.

## 15. Dates, order and formats

- **A card's date is today when it is made or switched on** (`createBusiness*`, `createLegalEntityExpense`: Start date today; ticking End date sets today, or the Start date if later). Turning a Payment Plan on gives the card a Start and an End date (today) where it has none, since the plan's dates fall between them.
- **The date defines the order, then the number** (`orderedByDate`, applied on every change of the Infrastructure & Capability page): each list of cards is kept in Start-date order (ties keep their place; no date goes last). The founders keep the Legal Entity's order.
- **A day is shown in the reader's date format everywhere** (`isoToDisplay` with the preference): the date cells (`DateCellInput`, now also the Registration Date, the Opening Date, the Legal Entity's expense Date, and the generic date field), the card tiles and the Registrations tile.
- **Where a list has a date, the date defines the order**, then the number: the Business cards (Start date) and the Legal Entity's expenses (Date).
- **The day in a date cell** is written at body size (`--font-body`), the size of the text around it.
- **One date field and one month field** (`DateCellInput`, `MonthYearPicker`), `--control-date` wide wherever asked (the calendar's funnel, the Notes panel, Global Settings included); no native date box. A note's actions wrap inside the note: the date cell goes to its own row where the row is narrow; nothing stands outside the note's box.
- **The Calendar lists every card date** (`CalendarCardDates.ts`) and has a funnel (`CalendarFunnel.tsx`): the period (Today, Tomorrow, This week, Next week, This month, This year, Xmas, the user's own) and the kinds of date (the recommended ones, all, or chosen).
- **Dates are limited where they can conflict**: an End date is not before its Start (a Start after the End takes the End along), a payment falls between Start and End, a warranty is not before the Start (`min` / `max` of `DateCellInput`).
- **A note's Date** is a tick; ticked, its label is the day to pick and the note stands on that day of the Calendar.

---

## 16. Texts and names

- **Text**: no em dash in any app text or source (`noEmDash.test.ts`).
- **Four free texts** (short text, list line, note, long text): Interface 3 holds the four categories and what the owner decided (a list line is one row by default, every optional text is labelled **Notes**, a long text is eight rows).
- **Words are written whole where the cell has room** ("% of Rev", "Per project", "Working Hours"); a short form ("rev.", "proj.") only for a label over 14 characters.
- **Notes (the side window)**: each note shows "Recorded <date and time>" (read-only, muted, 14px) under its text, and an "Add Date" text button beside "Link a field" (`.notepad-note-actions-text`) that adds today's date to the text on its own line. The Description of a card carries no stamp and no button.
- **What a note does to the part it links** (owner, 2026-11-10: "it should be written in the doc, the effect of taking a note (the colour and wireframe)"): a field, a card or a page's tile that a note links is drawn in the noted orange: a 4px stroke (`--line-noted`) just inside its edge, over its own border, so nothing moves; a gentle orange ground (`--bg-noted`); and a soft glow round it (`--glow-noted`, 12px), drawn inside where a box clips what it holds. It says "In your notes" to a screen reader (`markNoted`, fieldMarks.tsx; notepad.css).
- **Pointed at** (the part itself, or its note or one of its links in the Notes panel): the stroke steps outside the edge and the glow widens (16px, 4px spread); the note and its link chips light the same way. While the part holds the focus, the focus ring is drawn in place of the glow.
- **A link opens its part**: a press on a link chip goes to the page and opens what it names; a page's tile opens its dialog, as a press on it does; a field in a tile's dialog opens that dialog (the link keeps its tile, `tile`), is brought into view and lit for a moment. Pointing at a noted part in a dialog or a floating card lights its notes and shows its tooltip, as on the page. A lit note or chip is one frame (the stroke over its own border) (`openLink`, notepadPlaces.ts). A chip is the link's number over its name, its x inside it, 4px clear of its edge.
- **The Workbook's Card Notes list every card's Notes** under a funnel that chooses pages; a press opens the card (`CardNotesService`, `WorkbookCardNotes`; Interface 3). The Notes panel holds the person's own notes only.
- **The Card notes of the Notes panel are notes**: a note's box (12px padding, corners, ground), name, page and text 6px apart, no tracking on the text.
- **One Notes panel column**: the card notes and the notes of each Organisation stand in one scrolling column, 16px padded; a card note is a note's box (12px padding, 6px between its lines, glow on the pointer, ring on the focus).
- **The Notes panel's funnel is the neutral one** (the text colour on the panel's ground): the panel has no card hue to take its tone from, and the hue 0 it would fall back to is red.
- **The "i" of a category's dialog** (Resources, Partners, Suppliers) says the help text of its groups from the help-texts document: the text of its one group, or one line for each group ("Company Vehicles: cars and bikes ..."). Opening one group of a category shows that group's own text.
- **Names**: the Ambassador's "Attitude & Body Language" is "Body Language" (its saved key stays `attitude-body-language`).
- **A drop-down with nothing chosen says "Select"** (the Legal expense's Frequency and Responsible, the Strategy's Responsible).

## 17. Declared exceptions

- **Text Hierarchy box**: every row is one height, the room H1 needs at
  its largest size (lines at 1.25), 12px apart, so the px squares stand at
  one equal pitch and the box never changes size. Each square stands at its
  row's foot with its tick on its centre line. A name is centred on its
  square while no taller than it, then keeps its foot on the square's foot
  and grows upward only: never below its square, never into the row above
  (checked at every size of every range). The box takes 12px more inside
  each side (24px), and as much room under the last square as above the
  first (the first row's reserve is matched at the foot).
- **Colour and Typeface cards: the body under the name box.** Their rows
  (Role, Colour, Usage) start at the name box's left edge and end at its
  right edge, not at the number's x and the card's edge (the card is a
  grid whose head shares its columns). Role and Colour are side-label rows
  (`.brand-side-rows`): labels in one column on their controls' centre
  line, controls to the right edge; a colour's contrast is the first point
  of the Colour's "i". Every other card keeps the number-left rule; the
  check tests this exception as "body under the name box".
- **Composite groups** measured as one control: price + currency, swatch +
  HEX, a list row with its x, month side-label rows, the channel pick and
  resource rows, the founders table (see `GROUPS` in the probe).
- **Boxes that are not cards**: the Financial Foundation's settings bar,
  headline and tiles, a demand source and a channel resource keep their
  own inner margins.
- **Tables** (`table` elements, numbered lists, the Notepad) size their
  own cells.
- **A founder's card** (the Founders' dialog): its left padding is the card padding plus the inset and the marker column (4.5px + 26.3px), the head's number, the bullets, the fold arrows and the list numbers hang in that column (the "number-left = body-left" and "content starts at the title" rules are replaced by the founder-card rule of the check: text at the content edge, markers at the marker column, the head number 8px before the name). Its four cells are `--founder-cell` wide, a width of their own (the Frequency picker's longest option), whatever their column; the two hours labels are two lines. 
- **A side label whose control is the empty message** ("Add a founder under ...", a card of an organisation with no founder yet) is centred on that message, which may wrap to two lines: the row-gap rule skips it.
- **A card in a dialog, opened** (a card box pressed) is centred and `--open-card-width` (492px) wide, not stretched across the dialog.
- **A page tile under the pointer** stands above its neighbours (`z-index: 6`) at its own size, inside its card's body (since 2026-11-23 it no longer grows: section 8).
- **Pickers whose list is wider than their cell** (opened cards): the rule that a picker is as wide as its longest option, and shows its whole text, has these named exceptions besides the Status picker. A supplier or partner cascade's Group and Name pickers (a group name such as "Landlords, Leasing & Warehouses" is 322px with its padding; a name the user gave is any length), an activity's Team pickers (a person's name and role, or a partner's), and the Group picker of a Resource, Partner or Provider card. Each is as wide as its 212px cell and ends in an ellipsis there; the whole text is in its list, its tooltip and its accessible name. The check skips them by their accessible name ("... supplier group: ...", "... supplier: ...", "... performer N: ...", "... group: ...").
- **The cost row's Frequency column** (a card with no group yet offers all seven frequencies, "% of Rev" the longest, 148px): the column gives way to the room left (`minmax(0, max-content)`), so the Frequency ends at the name cell's right edge, up to 16px narrower. A Per unit price with a Unit picker puts the Frequency and the Unit together under the row (`:has(.business-capability-frequency-unit)`), the currency keeping its place.
- **An activity's Responsible stack**, which has no Team list and so no x, has two columns and ends at the name cell's right edge (the Team's stack keeps its third, the x's).
- **A box of bands** (`.business-segment-box`: the Segment Description and the Profile card): its questions are bands that follow each other flush, each with its own padding (section 8, "A floating card of questions", "Three levels" and "Flush"), not fields a row gap apart, and a label with its cell beside it (the Profile's age) has the cell at the right end of the band. The row-gap rule and the "control at its column's left edge" rule skip them; `npm run check:card` measures the bands.
- **A group band in a floating card** (`.fold-group-head`, such as a Competitor's Description) runs from side to side of the card: the "name cell left = body-left" rule measures the body from the first part after the band.

## 18. The check

**The progress line** (the thin bar under the Hide Help button) is hidden for now; one switch, `SHOW_PROGRESS_LINE` in `src/components/systems/InterviewProgress.tsx` (false). No check or rule depends on it, and no gap is left behind.

`tools/scripts/layout-check/layout-probe.js` measures a page in the browser;
`tools/scripts/layout-check/run.mjs` opens every Foundation page of both examples
(`src/data/examples`), every card unfolded, runs the probe and the hover check, and
lists the failures. **A finding that is not in `tools/scripts/layout-check/baseline.json`
fails it; the baseline is empty, so any finding fails.**
Among its rules (2026-11-14): **a dialog's band meets both of its edges** -- a band of a dialog's body that starts at its left edge reaches its right edge too.
**The probe's rules changed for the stacked page (2026-11-20, each confirmed on a 768 screenshot):** (1) the page's rails are read only while they show: they are hidden while a layer is open (section 10), and a hidden rail had read a 97px gap; (2) a dialog's expected top on a scrolled page is 12px under the page title's underline, its height to the frame's or the window's foot (section 23); (3) every tile's scene starts with the window at its top and the tile brought into view, the scroll settled, and `checkScrolledDialog` is a scene of its own; (4) a card over two chapters one under the other is measured against the chapter its dialog covers, and "opens on its column's first row" is read only with the window unscrolled (its rest place is the next rule's, which undoes the scroll); (5) Infrastructure's two columns trade cards only side by side (the app's `otherColumn` too). (6) The hover check gives the window's scroll back after bringing page tiles into view (it had scrolled the page under an open layer, 600px at 768). Reason: below 1200px the chapters stack and the page is taller than the window; the probe had read the moved rows of a scrolled window, and a layout fixed for the reader.
The runs:

- `npm run check:layout`: the quick run, the one setting the baseline is made at (the Quillfield example and the Foundation page of the proxy-tutoring fixture, 1920 wide, the light page).
- `node tools/scripts/layout-check/run.mjs`: the full run, at **1920, 1536, 1366 and 768px** (1536: a 1920 screen at 125% scaling, the second most common desktop width, StatCounter; added 2026-11-20 15:35 UTC+2) in **light and dark**, both examples and the fixture (about 25 minutes). The fixture (`tools/scripts/layout-check/fixtures/proxy-tutoring.json`, a parent buying tutoring for a child) is check-only: no test reads it and the app never ships it; it is there so the checks draw the Profile's two parts, "The buyer" and "The end user". `npm run check:card` audits its Profile as well as the Considered Nester.
- `npm run check:layout:wide`: the three reading modes and the screen classes; its baseline is not written yet.
- `CHECK_PAGES="Customer Engagement" npm run check:layout` runs one page; a sub-pixel finding (a gap of 12.95 for 12) can come and go between runs. A card added with a "+" box is waited for (two seconds at most) until it is drawn and has taken the focus (in its name, or on the card where the app writes the name), so a slow frame on a busy machine is not a finding.
- `npm run check:layout:baseline` rewrites the baseline: only by the owner's decision.

Rules checked: number-left = body-left; number to name gap (8); control at
its column's left edge; same-row tops; list x column; add button spans its
list; side-by-side bottoms; text box not clipped and at least two lines;
picker one line; nothing overflows its card or the page; text beside a
control on its centre line; tick box on its words' first line; an "i" on its words' line, 6px (`--info-gap`) after them; a hierarchy
level on its row's centre line; control width is a size step; single-line
control height; card side margin; page grid column, row and box gaps (12); label gap (6) and row gap (12, or 24
across a dividing rule) in every card; section tiles (a square of `--section-tile-size` -- a third of the group's width less the gaps, one size per card, three across fill the group --, title centred, 12px apart, left-aligned) and everything inside each tile's opened dialog; and the hover reactions above.

The check starts the dev server itself when none answers (port 1420) and stops it at the end; Playwright is a dev dependency. The procedure around it is in `03-toolchain-and-verification-playbook.md` (step 5).

- **From an agent's Playwright browser tool**: evaluate `run.mjs`'s
  functions with `page` bound (strip its imports and `export`s, fetch the
  probe from `/tools/scripts/layout-check/layout-probe.js`, the examples from
  `/src/data/examples/`), then call `runLayoutCheck(page, { probe })` for each
  seeded example. The check writes the examples into localStorage, and the app moves them into its IndexedDB at its next start (merged by id, `IndexedDbStorage.ts`):
  run it in a browser profile of its own, or delete the examples afterwards.

**The real mouse.** `checkRealMouse(page, where)` in `run.mjs` (called for Infrastructure & Capability at 1366 x 1000, 1366 x 700 and 1920 x 1000) presses every list item of every first-row tile, the page scrolled to its top, with real mouse events (move, down, up): the item must not move 0.5px between the press and the release, its card or dialog must open, Escape must return to the default page (no tile grown, no ring on a mouse-opened opener), and one Enter-opened item must keep its ring on closing with no tile grown. (The old code fails it: 38 failures at 1600 x 780.)

**A long tab in a short window.** `checkLongRail(page, base, where)` in `run.mjs` (called for the Strategy System's page) makes thirteen Themes, opens the last one's dialog at 1366 x 768 and at 768 x 1000, and fails where the page stops or the Theme's dialog is not open, or where the tab's top is not the same over a second; the Organisation is put back as it was. (The old code fails it: the page stopped at 1366 x 768, 2026-11-21.)

**The Capability pages' arrows.** `checkCapabilityArrows(page, base)` in `run.mjs` (once a run, with the first example, where no page was picked) opens each Capability's page at 1870 x 1000 and 1366 x 768 and fails one whose Systems stand in two rows with its page arrow's centre more than 2px from the middle of the gap between them ("a Capability page's arrows stand between its rows of Systems").

**Section tiles.** The page is measured as drawn, then
`await window.__layoutProbe.checkWithTiles(spec)` (what `run.mjs` calls) opens each
`.section-tile` in turn, unfolds the dialog ("Expand all", then any arrow
still folded), runs the same checks with the dialog open (each failure names
its `tile`), and closes it with Back. Call it instead of `check(spec)` on any page
with tiles; on a page without, it is the plain check.

The probe's pure helpers are unit-tested in `src/test/layoutProbe.test.ts`.

- **Probe**: hover is held (`holdHover`) so tile-geometry rules ignore the 110% scale; the dialog rules compare with the section card's rect; several Add tiles may end a grid; an empty group tile (`section-tile-slot-empty`, 50%) may list nothing.
- **Layout probe**: a remove x keeps 12px from the cell beside it and never lies on one.

### The stylesheets stay clean

`npm run lint:css` fails when a stylesheet holds a rule for a class that no component draws, or a declaration that a later one of the same selector always overrides (`tools/scripts/lint/css-usage.mjs`, `css-shadowed.mjs`; the flag remove, with a file, deletes what they list). `node tools/scripts/catalogue/style-snapshot.mjs` with the flag out and a file name writes the box and the computed style of every element of every page, opened tile and first card, in light and dark at 1920px and light at 768px (about 107,000 elements); the flag compare, with two snapshots, lists what differs between two snapshots, so a clean-up can be proved to change nothing. How the stylesheets are laid out: the custom properties of the app are in one TOKENS section at the top of global.css, in seven groups; conversation.css and section-tiles.css are lists of imports over a folder of the same name, one file per topic, in the order of the old single file (the cascade follows that order, so a rule is moved between files only on purpose); a relative url() in those files is one folder deeper. A test or a tool reads such a sheet with its imports in place (tools/scripts/lint/css-imports.ts). A few elements move between two runs of the same code (the Registrations tile of Identity: Organisation, the carousel arrows at 768px): those are timing, not the change.

## 19. The assets (generated from the contracts)

The numbers each shared part's contract sets (the assets of `src/library`, measured by `npm run library` in light, dark and sepia at 768, 1440 and 1920 px wide) are in [10-layout-assets.md](10-layout-assets.md), written from the contracts by `npm run docs:assets` and checked by `npm run docs:check`; this rulebook does not repeat them.

- **Incomplete-card flashing** does not cover Customer Engagement's channel and subchannel cards or the Founders Profile cards (rule 11).

- **Hide Help**: the page of 3. Infrastructure & Capability has none; the other pages keep their text button, which starts open.
- **The completed card's dropdown chevron** is drawn in the theme's ink, not in `--completed-dim` (a fifth tone in a card of four greys).

## 23. One shape for every page of chapters

- A page's frames start and end on the page title's underline, edge to edge, and are centred with it. Frames of one page are 12px apart (`.page-group` for a page of several sections, the Business page's grid for its two).
- A frame: 12px padding, 8px corners, one hairline edge in its hue, its heading on top in capitals, centred, 28px semi-bold (`.answer-column-label`, `.tiles-frame-head`).

**Every chapter is drawn alike** (owner, 2026-11-19 22:47 UTC+2, the two Lumen Segment chapters: chapter 2's tile text was smaller). A chapter's tiles take their side, gap and type (18px lists from 250px a side) from the first chapter SHOWN, and fit again when a chapter is shown (`SectionTiles`, a ResizeObserver): a chapter hidden by the carousel is 0px wide and never sets its own. **Every dialog opens at its frame's top** (owner, 2026-11-19 23:15 UTC+2: "The dialogue cards' level are different on the Segment. Fix it."): a dialog's top is the top of the frame as it shows (`dialogFrame`, `shownFrame`): a Segment's chapter, its title band included, as the page's frame on every other page; its sides and foot are its section's. **On a page scrolled past that top** (a window under 1200px, where the stacked chapters run below the window), the dialog stands where a frame stands at rest, 12px under the page title's underline, never against it, and its tab 12px under the dialog's top, the tab counting only the scroll since the dialog opened (`titleTopOf`) (2026-11-20; rule | was | now: a dialog opens at its frame's top | scrolled, the dialog stood against the underline, 12px higher than at rest, and the layout check expected the frame's hidden top | 12px under the underline, the rest place; `dialogFrame`, the layout probe). Floating cards keep their own level (section 8, "Placed on the first row", owner 13:45). The layout check shows each chapter in turn at 1920 x 1000 and 2560 x 1200 and compares its tiles' side, gap and type with the first's (`checkChapters`), and opens every master tile of each page, its dialog's top against its frame's (`checkDialogLevel`).

**The chapter hugs its master tiles at every width** (owner, 2026-11-22 20:38 UTC+2, option A): its padding round them; side by side, the tiles as large as the window's height and the chapter's column allow, two chapters 12px apart, the pair centred, one chapter centred; the spare width is margin outside the frames (`SectionTiles`, `chapterOf`).

**Master Tiles never change their sizes, and layout.** The master tiles of a chapter (the tiles a page's frame holds) are one size in a window whatever their number: one or two stand in one row, three or four in the 2 x 2 (three leave its fourth place empty, `SectionTiles.tsx`, `threeLarge`), each min((the box's width - the gap) / 2, `--quad-max-side`) a side, the same on every chapter of every page. No change may resize or reflow them: a change adds or moves content inside them. The layout probe checks it (17a: every master tile of a page has that one side). `--quad-max-side` is what the window leaves under the page's FIRST chapter, for every chapter of the page (in the vertical layout it is not set by the height: the three steps below): where the chapters stand one under another (768px), a lower chapter keeps the first's size (the full layout check, 2026-11-14, found Customer Engagement's second customer group at the 150px minimum).

**The vertical layout's title, tiles and arrows** (owner, 2026-11-22 18:58 UTC+2, his screenshots 6 and 12: "When the screen became narrower, the page name should go Under the System Name. The em dash between them do not need to exist anymore. The Icon should stay at the System's level. Also, the Chapter frame, and the master Tiles, should shrink or grow proportionally. The chapter frame should always hug (having a specific gap) the master tiles"; "The navigation arrow when exist, should stay in place"; rule | was | now: one shape for every page of chapters | below 1100px or in a portrait window the page title kept "System — Page" on one run that wrapped anywhere, the master tiles kept the side the window's height allowed in a chapter as wide as the page (a wide empty margin round them), and the page arrows stood at a level worked out from the title's lines, so they moved from page to page | the page's title on a line of its own under the System's name, no dash (`.interview-header-dash`), the icon level with the System's name; the master tiles in three steps and the chapter frame HUGGING them, its own 12px padding round them, centred under the page title (owner, 19:37 UTC+2: "Build 1-3 for Vertical Lay out", after research on how tablet and phone apps use their space: Android's window size classes and Material's grid add columns as a window widens and keep items at a comfortable size, Apple's HIG asks that an iPad's extra room hold content): 1. a phone (600px and under), the 2 x 2 fills the width; 2. up to 879px, the 2 x 2 grows with the width up to the desktop's side (`--quad-cap`, 330px); 3. from 880px (`ROW_OF_FOUR_QUERY`, `useMasterAcross`), the master tiles of a chapter in ONE ROW OF FOUR, up to the same side, three leaving the fourth place empty (`SectionTiles`, `chapterOf`, `--quad-across`; the side not held to the window's height where the page scrolls); the page arrows at the middle of the window between the header and the footer on every page (`arrowLevel`)). **A System of one page has no page arrow** (owner, same message: "If a system consists of only a single page there is no need for navigation arrow"): its rail's Introduction is one press away.

**Every tile is a square, at every width**: a master tile on a page, a sub tile, a card tile and an add box inside a dialog; the grid sets the width and the height is the same. A window too narrow for the usual size makes the squares smaller, never oblong. The Inventory and Completed row's rectangles are no tiles of a grid (section 12). **The decided exception (Thanos, 2026-11-09):** the Strategic Period's Roadmap keeps its Objectives as timeline bars, as long as their period (441 x 100 at 1920, 69 x 100 at 768); the probe skips them. The probe checks every other tile at each width it measures: measured square on every page and dialog of both examples at 1920, 1366 and 768 (2026-11-09).

The chapters (the page's cards) and the master tiles in them are the same on every page of a window: the cards stand side by side in a window of 1100px or wider that is wider than it is tall, and stack, one to a row, in any other (ONE query, `(max-width: 1099px), (orientation: portrait)`, 07-floating-cards-and-page-layout.css: `.page-group`, `.segment-chapter-groups`, Infrastructure & Capability's two cards, `--one-chapter-width`; the same words in `useVerticalLayout.ts`), on Infrastructure & Capability as on Brand, Strategy, Identity (**the vertical layout**, owner, 2026-11-22 09:52 UTC+2: "In case a screen gets narrower, or it is no longer a wide-shaped one. Change the page layout from horizontal to vertical. So the chapters are one on top of the other"; 09:58 UTC+2, "1100px, landscape only"; rule | was | now: one line for every page | the chapters stood side by side from 1200px whatever the window's shape, landscape iPads (1180 x 820, 1194 x 834) stacked, and Infrastructure & Capability kept its two cards side by side down to 834px, its second title wrapping at 1024 | side by side from 1100px in a landscape window, stacked below it and in every portrait window, Infrastructure & Capability too; measured 2026-11-22 on all six pages at 1024 to 1376 wide (the iPad sizes from iosref.com/res): from 1100px no chapter title wraps, no card runs under the footer, nothing scrolls sideways, master tiles 198 to 243px); **in the vertical layout a dialog's tiles and boxes stand two a row, a phone's too** (one a row on a phone until 2026-11-22 20:35 UTC+2, when the owner's screenshot of a phone's Profiles tiles, each the window's width, asked to "fix ... the dialogue tile sizes. Also check the other tiles") (owner, 2026-11-22 09:58 UTC+2: "Two a row on tablet, one on phone"; `TileGrid` through `useVerticalAcross`, the section dialogs' `.section-tiles-box` in the one query; the Strategic Period's Themes keep their three, section 8; measured at 768 x 1024: every dialog's tiles 309px, two a row; at 390 x 844: 252px, one a row), and a floating card stands over the chapter its dialog covers, never beside it (section 8, "Placed on the first row"); **below 1100px or in a portrait window a chapter of two groups (a Segment's, the Financials') stacks its groups the same way, one under the other, the line between them across, and a page of one chapter (Strategy) takes the page's whole width** (owner, 2026-11-20 15:23 UTC+2, option A: at 768 these pages' Master Tiles were 142px, the other pages' about 310px; 15:35 UTC+2, the line at 1200px (the website's tablet/desktop line, 1200px in its stylesheet (NorthStardustStudioWebsite); Android's window size classes, the large class from 1200dp, developer.android.com/develop/adaptive-apps/guides/use-window-size-classes); rule | was | now: the same window gives the same tiles | the page's chapters stacked below 1100px, two groups side by side and a lone chapter at the left at every width, their tiles a quarter of the window at 768 | all three stack below 1200px, so every page's tiles are one size in a window; nothing changes from 1200px). **The phone** (600px and under, ONE query, `(max-width: 600px)`, `PHONE_QUERY` and `isPhone()` in `useVerticalLayout.ts`; owner, 2026-11-22 09:52 UTC+2: "for smaller screens (mobiles), the texts, tiles and cards sizes should be adjusted to fit"; rule | was | now: the minimum width was 768px | below 768 nothing was fitted: the rails stood over the cards, the dialogs held two cramped columns, the header ran out of the window | the app works from 320px wide, the phone rules below): the page card, every dialog and every floating card stand 16px in from the window's sides (`--rail-gutter: 16px`), one width; the headings step down (`--font-h1` 28px, `--font-h2` 24px, `--font-h3` 18px; body and labels keep their sizes); the header keeps the logo (32px high, smaller on the narrowest phones), the save signal, the Menu and the Workbook, the Organisation's name giving way to the page title; master tiles stand two a row as everywhere (160px at 390, 145px at 360, 125px at 320), their titles in the small size; the Inventory and Completed rectangles share the card's width; a dialog's or card's fields stand in one column (the Organisation's two columns, a founder's Name and Capital); **the page's rails, a layer's rail and their arrows stand in one band across the window's foot** (64px, the header's ground and shadow: Back at the left, "‹", the dots 18px apart, "›" at the right; `bullet-rail.css`, `12-page-tabs-and-channel-card.css`; the decision card of 2026-11-22, "In the footer band", recommended), and every layer, scrolled to its end, ends above it. Measured 2026-11-22 on all six pages, every master tile's dialog and the floating cards at 390 x 844, 360 x 780 and 320 x 640: nothing scrolls sideways, no field grid holds two columns, every layer clears the band. The 400% zoom of WCAG 2.2 Reflow (1.4.10) is a 320 CSS px window, so it is the phone layout. the four tiles of a card are one size by the window's width and height, the room of the Inventory and Completed row (the squeezed row, 56px and its gap) being kept under the cards on EVERY page, whether the page has the row or not, so that the same window gives the same tiles. The gap between the tiles is 12px at every window height (owner, 2026-11-20 21:01 and 21:07 UTC+2; rule | was | now: the 2 x 2 tiles are 12px apart, as section 9, "Page cards", and the probe say | this sentence let the gap narrow, down to 6px, where the window's height made the tiles under 85% of what the width would give (7px at 1920 x 1100), so a tile grown under the pointer covered much of its neighbours | 12px always; the layout check, with a 1366 x 768 window added to its standard windows, gives 0 findings on every page of the three examples; the probe fails a chapter whose master tiles are not equal squares on the 2 x 2 grid, 12px apart, "the master tiles of a chapter are equal squares on the shared 2 x 2 grid"). **One chapter size on every page** (owner, 2026-11-11; 2026-11-20 21:33 UTC+2: "fix the size of the Financial Tile and its chapter"): from 1200px a chapter of a 2 x 2 is half the page card less 12px, one chapter or two (rule | was | now: one size of chapter and tile in a window | the Financials' one chapter, made to span the page while it held two groups, stood 1400px wide at 1920 with 376px tiles | a chapter as Strategy's, 694px, 328px tiles; the probe fails "a chapter of master tiles is one size on every page"; a Segment's chapter, two groups in one frame, is its own kind). The rectangular tiles (Inventory, Completed) have no rule under their title.
